> Historical depth, brackets, continuous improvement

- [~] **Full historical data support across the site** (NatStat perfs back to 2007, ~20 seasons). **2015-2026 ingested (12 seasons) as of 2026-05-17** — 2022-2026 via the API path; 2015-2021 via the new `bootstrap-csv` subcommand (see Refactor Backlog). 2007-2014 remain — same CSV path works, just needs the dashboard exports. Per Phase 3 notes, expanding the cstat training window remains a high-leverage lever for the predict model, though the diminishing-returns inflection point above 5 seasons is unproven empirically. Pre-2015 brings sharper era-effect risk for combined-cohort archetypes (see `docs/archetypes_methodology.md` "Era horizon") — three-point line moved in 2008, small-ball/3PT-volume era began ~2014.
  - **Data availability**: `/seasons` confirms perfs for 2007-2026 (20 seasons), play-by-play from 2012+. Each season ≈ 6,200 games, ≈ 6,000 players, ≈ 110k box scores. Rate-limited at 500 API calls/hr → full backfill is a multi-day job leaning on the existing `api_cache` table.
  - **Ingest**: extend `cstat-ingest season` to accept a year range and run the full pipeline (teams → games → perfs → teamperfs → forecasts → elo) per season. Handle historical conference realignment, team renames, and defunct programs without breaking FK constraints. Layer Torvik backfill on the same range (CSV is per-year).
  - **Compute**: run the 13-step compute pipeline per historical season. CamPom, percentiles, adj efficiency, and archetypes are all already season-scoped, but worth sanity-checking early seasons where some advanced fields (e.g., shot zones from Torvik) may be missing.
  - **Schema**: confirm `(season, …)` indices are present and effective at 20× current data volume; spot-check query plans for cross-season joins. Postgres should handle the size fine — main risk is unindexed fan-out on player career queries.
  - [x] **API**: every endpoint already accepts a `season` query param with `default_season()` fallback (`crates/cstat-api/src/main.rs:24`). The `season` plumbs through `features.rs::build_game_features` so even the predict model handles arbitrary historical matchups. Career-aggregate endpoints (`/api/players/:id/career`, `/api/teams/:id/history`) and cross-season `/api/players/compare` are still future work — they need new query shapes once we have more seasons to compare across.

Predict page already supports historical back-testing via the same selector. Adding a new season is a one-line change to `AVAILABLE_SEASONS` in `season.ts` once data lands. Multi-season *career trajectories* on player/team detail pages are still future work — they need the historical seasons ingested first.
  - **ML**: retrain on all seasons (incremental: 2024 → 2023 → … to measure marginal lift per season added). Watch for distribution shift (rule changes, three-point line move in 2008, COVID-shortened 2021).
  - **Archetypes at scale**: combined-cohort training works at 2-3 seasons; degrades around 5+ when era effects (3PT volume, small-ball, rule changes) make players from different eras non-comparable. See `docs/archetypes_methodology.md` for trigger criteria and candidate strategies (sliding window, era-bucketed clustering, per-decade models).

- [x] **Freshman recruiting ingest + tier-mean heuristic + per-player prior model**. The /projections page now consumes a third roster source. **Ingest shipped: 5,922 recruits across 13 classes (class-of-2014 through class-of-2026) as of 2026-05-18. Tier-mean heuristic shipped: 4-tier population profile keyed on composite_rank. Per-player freshman-impact model shipped (PR #66) and dramatically expanded 2026-05-18 — corpus grew 1,154 → 3,252 rows after pre-2021 recruit backfill, LOCO MAE 2.462 → 2.258 (8.3% lift / 6.5% above baseline).**

  **✓ Shipped — recruit ingest pipeline** (migration 020 + `Recruit247Client` + ingest module + CLI):
  - `recruits` table with `(year, recruit_key)` UNIQUE on 247's stable player ID; columns for composite_rank / composite_rating / star_rating / previous_rank / position_rank / state_rank, position / height / weight, hometown city/state, high_school, committed_school + slug + `committed_team_id UUID REFERENCES teams(id)`, commit_status, profile/photo URLs, `raw_player JSONB` escape valve, `cstat_player_id UUID REFERENCES players(id)` resolved post-arrival. 5 indexes mirroring transfers.

New piece is the `scraper`-based HTML parser `parse_recruits_html` keyed to `li.rankings-page__list-item`. Four commit-state variants handled: img-link+checkmark = "Signed", img-link alone = "Committed", bare `<img>` direct child of `.status` (schools without 247 landing pages, e.g. Cal Baptist) = "Committed" without slug, `.rankings-page__crystal-ball` = "Uncommitted".

Pass 2 (`resolve_player_joins`) matches `(full_name, committed_team_natstat_id, season=year+1)` → `players.id`; cheap idempotent no-op until cstat-season `year+1` box scores ingest.

  - **Historical ingest** (class-of-2024, class-of-2025): 321 + 512 = 833 recruits. 303/307 + 465/472 team resolution. 224 + 334 = 558 `cstat_player_id` resolutions against the already-ingested 2024-26 freshmen — the empirical (recruit-rank, actual-freshman-stats) corpus that powers the tier-mean heuristic. Auth: 247 changed away from a bare `JWT={value}` cookie at some point; client now accepts a full session-cookie string via `TFS_247_COOKIE` (DevTools Copy-as-cURL), falling back to legacy `TFS_247_JWT` for backward compat. See `crates/cstat-ingest/src/tfs_recruits.rs::Recruit247Client`.
  - [x] **`recruit_key_from_url` URL-format fix** (issue #146 refresh fallout). 247 began appending a `/high-school-{schoolId}/?Sport=2` suffix to recruit profile URLs (mid-2026). The old trailing-number regex (`-(\d+)/?$`) then captured the **school** ID (or matched nothing when `?Sport=2` was present), silently re-keying every recruit and **inserting duplicate rows on refresh** (a class-of-2026 re-ingest doubled the table: 367 → 731). Re-anchored the regex to the `/player/{slug}-{id}` segment (`/player/[^/]*-(\d+)`) so the player ID is captured regardless of trailing path. Added URL-format test cases. After the fix, a clean class-of-2026 refresh restores 367 deduped rows.

  **✓ Shipped — tier-mean freshman heuristic** (Phase 5b §5b plug-in, see `docs/projections_methodology.md`):
  > **SUPERSEDED & DELETED 2026-06-05 (PRs #108/#109).** The entire 4-tier scaffold — `FreshmanTier`, the `{T1..T4}_PROFILE` constants, `tier_from_predicted_campom`, and the tier-reassignment path in `synthesize_freshman_row` — was **deleted from the codebase**. It collapsed to `roster_projection.rs::freshman_row(recruit_id, predicted_cam_v3) -> PlayerRow`, which reads the freshman-impact model's per-recruit CamPom directly. **Why it was safe**: the *served* projection (roster-impact model) reaches a recruit row through only three fields (`cam_v3`, `class_year`, `primary_class`); the synthesized box-score statline reached no served model, so the deletion was byte-identical on `/api/projections/2027` (max diff 3.8e-06 float32 noise). Whole-batch inference failure falls back to `FRESHMAN_FALLBACK_CAM_V3 = +1.20` (the training-target mean), replacing the old per-tier-mean fallback. See memory `[[project_freshman_tiers_deprecated]]`. The tier-era detail below is kept for historical context only.
  - 4-tier bucketing on `composite_rank`: T1 (1–30) / T2 (31–100) / T3 (101–250) / T4 (251+ or unranked). Sample sizes per tier: 52 / 114 / 201 / 185.
  - Per-tier mean profile (MPG, GP, PPG, rate stats, CamPom v3) lives as `T{1..4}_PROFILE` constants in `crates/cstat-core/src/roster_projection.rs`. Refresh by re-running the calibration query when a new class lands.
  - `synthesize_freshman_row(recruit_id, tier) -> PlayerRow` plugs each commit into `build_roster_features` like any other player.
  - Headline CamPom v3 monotonicity: T1 +8.97 / T2 +2.41 / T3 +0.70 / T4 −0.57. T3 vs T4 are nearly indistinguishable — composite rank stops being a strong signal past ~100.
  - **Honesty caveat**: tier-mean is a population average, not a per-player projection. A 5★ who busts and a 5★ All-American both project as +8.97 CamPom. The per-player upgrade is the prior model below.

  **Schema deltas from the original plan** (already reflected in `migrations/020_recruits.sql`):
  - Cross-season join uses `committed_team_id UUID REFERENCES teams(id)` and re-resolves via `natstat_id` at query time (mirrors transfers). The originally-planned separate `committed_team_natstat_id` column was unnecessary.
  - Added `previous_rank` for movement tracking (247 exposes prior-period rank via `.rank-column .other`).
  - Added `committed_school_slug` extracted from college URL; dropped `last_update_date` (not exposed in the HTML).
  - `commit_status` left without CHECK constraint until vocab was confirmed; now confirmed as `Signed` / `Committed` / `Uncommitted` after the live ingest — tightening CHECK is a one-line follow-up if desired.

  **Empirical finding worth flagging**: 247's `compositerecruitrankings` endpoint returns identical content for all `InstitutionGroup` values (`highschool` / `juco` / `prep`) when called with only the subscriber `JWT` cookie. CLI defaults to `--groups highschool`; the `Juco` / `Prep` enum values are kept so the schema vocab is ready for future endpoints once we find the right URLs for those cohorts. See `crates/cstat-ingest/src/tfs_recruits.rs::InstitutionGroup` for the parser-level note.

  **Remaining work** (separate PRs, in rough priority order):
  - [x] **Freshman-impact prior model** *(shipped — served on the projection path; LOCO MAE 2.258 after the 2014–2020 backfill)* — the year-0 → year-1 per-player projection (upgrade from the tier-mean heuristic above). **Training pipeline shipped** (`training/train_freshman_model.py`). LightGBM mean + q10/q90 on 963 qualified freshman rows (≥5 GP / ≥5 MPG) across **four classes (class-of-2022 → 2023 through class-of-2025 → 2026)**. 13 features: 11 from the shared recruit-feature extractor + 2 freshman-specific (`committed_team_prior_adjem`, `peer_class_strength`). School-context features skip the dog-fooding trap by reading committed-team AdjEM from the season BEFORE the recruit arrives. **Result vs. tier-mean baseline**: pooled MAE 2.561 → **2.488** (−2.9%); T1 (top-30 ranked, n=110) 4.318 → **3.840** (−11.1%); T4 (unranked, n=290) 2.217 → **2.036** (−8.2%); T2/T3 nearly flat (±0.05 MAE). R² 0.306 → 0.367. The school-context features rank #1 and #2 by importance — committed-team prior AdjEM and peer-class strength are the two biggest gains beyond the recruit-direct block. Artifacts: `training/models/freshman_{mean,q10,q90}_model.onnx` + `freshman_model_meta.json`. **Selection-bias caveat is even sharper here than for the trajectory model**: elite freshmen leave for the draft, so the model is calibrated on returners-who-played-meaningful-minutes, not the full draft-eligible cohort. Future-Boozer top-30 projections inherit looser bands than the headline MAE suggests; the q10/q90 band is the honest framing.
  - [x] **Rust inference for freshman model** — shipped. `crates/cstat-core/src/freshman_model.rs` mirrors the trajectory pattern: `FRESHMAN_NUM_FEATURES = 13` (11 shared recruit block + 2 school-context tail), `fetch_freshman_features(pool, recruit_id) -> Option<FreshmanFeatureRow>` (single recruit lookup with the season-before-arrival AdjEM traversal + peer-class subquery), `build_freshman_features(row) -> [f32; 13]` (delegates the first 11 slots to `recruit_features::build_recruit_feature_block`, appends the 2 school-context features). `Predictor` carries 3 new sessions; `validate_freshman_meta` mirrors `validate_trajectory_meta` (same `player_filter` / feature-list / quantile-alpha contract checks). New `LoadError::FreshmanMetaMismatch` variant. Dockerfile copies the 3 ONNX files + meta JSON into the runtime image. Boot-time validator hard-fails on drift. Tests: 4 unit tests (feature-vector layout, school-context sentinel branch, missing-row sentinel branch, contract cross-check vs `RECRUIT_FEATURE_NAMES`) + 1 integration smoke test that loads ONNX + predicts a mid-tier ranked recruit and asserts finite predictions within plausible CamPom range.
  - [x] **Δ-rank column on the Recruits tab** — shipped. Mirrors `TransferPortal`'s pattern: client-side computes `campom_rank` (recruit's position when class is sorted by projected freshman CamPom desc) and `rank_delta = composite_rank − campom_rank`. Positive (green) = CamPom rates the recruit higher than 247 does, negative (red) = CamPom is bearish on a scout favorite. NULL when either rank input is missing. **In-sample 2025 calibration check** showed the asymmetry is informative: negative-Δs are mostly correct bearish calls (Joshua Lewis #54 → projected −1.55, actual −1.04; Nigel Walls #69 → projected −0.10, actual −0.03); positive-Δs are mixed but the model's lifts are appropriately conservative (max +4 for unknowns, doesn't claim elite). Δ is a model-validation surface as much as a UI affordance.
  - [x] **Leave-one-class-out CV for the freshman model** — shipped. `leave_one_class_out_cv()` in `training/train_freshman_model.py` retrains once per held-out class, scores the held-out cohort against a tier-mean baseline built from train-only tier means (so the comparison stays honest — held-out class doesn't seed its own baseline). Always runs as part of the training pipeline; results land in `freshman_model_meta.json::loco_cv`. **Numbers from the current 4-class corpus** (n=963 pooled): per-fold MAE 2.316 / 2.681 / 2.558 / 2.554 (holding out 2022 / 2023 / 2024 / 2025 respectively); pooled MAE **2.534** vs tier-mean baseline **2.561** — model wins by 1.1% out-of-sample, vs 2.9% in-sample CV. Gap to 5-fold random CV is only **+0.046**, well within "no severe overfit" territory. Model wins 3 of 4 holdouts; 2023 ties baseline (cohort-specific patterns that don't generalize cleanly from 2022/2024/2025 — would smooth out with class-of-2021-and-earlier backfill). The takeaway: school-context features are doing real work but the bulk of the in-sample lift was mild overfit. Honest framing in any future surface should quote 1.1% out-of-sample, not 2.9%.
  - [x] **Per-recruit CamPom surface on the Recruits tab** — shipped. `GET /api/recruits/{year}` (`crates/cstat-api/src/routes/recruits.rs`) extended with `projected_campom_{mean,lower,upper}` for every recruit. The route's batch SQL inlines the freshman-model's school-context joins (committed-team prior AdjEM + per-(year, committed_team) peer-class subquery) so all features arrive in one query; per-row `build_freshman_features` + `Predictor::predict_freshman` adds ~3 ONNX runs per recruit (~370 recruits × 3 ≈ 1110 ONNX runs per class-of-N response, single-row through the mutex-serialized sessions; subsequently replaced by the batch-inference iteration below). Inference errors degrade gracefully — log the failure and emit NULL projection fields rather than failing the whole route. Frontend (`web/src/components/RecruitClass.tsx`) renders a new `Projection` column with a CamPom-tier-colored chip showing the mean, sorted desc by default; tooltip surfaces the mean + q10–q90 band + tier label. Coverage: 100% of recruit rows get a projection (pre-college features only — doesn't depend on `cstat_player_id` resolution). Live smoke against class-of-2026 returned plausible top-end projections (Stokes +12.65, Holt +11.90, Smith Jr +13.24) with wide bands (~+3 to +18 CamPom) consistent with the documented selection-bias caveat for elite recruits. Class-of-2025 backtest shows expected underprojection on top recruits (Dybantsa proj +13.45 vs actual +21.89, Boozer proj +15.73 vs actual +30.54) — the model's "calibrated on returners" framing makes these gaps explainable in the tooltip rather than misleading.
  - [x] **Batch inference for the recruits route** — shipped. `Predictor::predict_freshman_batch(rows: &[[f32; FRESHMAN_NUM_FEATURES]]) -> Result<Vec<FreshmanPrediction>, ort::Error>` builds one `[N, 13]` tensor per model and unpacks N outputs from each — 3 ONNX runs total per route response regardless of N (was 3×N before). The recruits route (`crates/cstat-api/src/routes/recruits.rs`) now collects all feature vectors first, dispatches one batch call, and zips results back with the recruit rows. Failure semantics: batch-level error degrades to NULL projection fields for the whole response (logged once at warn level) — same UI behavior as the per-row null branch. Empty-input short-circuit returns `Ok(vec![])` before touching ONNX (avoids zero-row tensor edge cases). New `freshman_batch_matches_single_row` test proves batch outputs equal per-row outputs to fp precision; new `freshman_batch_empty_input` test pins the short-circuit. **Measured latency** (release build, local Postgres): /api/recruits/2026 (367 recruits) 8–12 ms, /api/recruits/2025 (512 recruits) 15–18 ms. Dominant cost is now SQL — inference is a small fraction. Same shape applies cleanly to the projection-page swap below (which loops over a team's full incoming class).
  - [x] **Projection-page swap — replace tier-mean in `synthesize_freshman_row`** — shipped. **Approach**: closest-impact-tier reassignment (not linear scaling — naive ratio scaling sign-flips when the predicted CamPom and tier mean span both signs). `synthesize_freshman_row(recruit_id, rank_tier, predicted_campom)` returns `(PlayerRow, FreshmanTier)`; when a prediction is available, the function picks the tier whose `cam_v3` centroid is closest to the prediction and synthesises from THAT tier's profile, while setting `cam_v3` on the row to the continuous prediction (so any downstream consumer that reads it directly — e.g. `roster_features::normalize_rotation` — gets the model's resolution, not the tier mean). Falls back to `rank_tier` when prediction unavailable (batch failure path). New `tier_from_predicted_campom(predicted)` helper. **Plumbing**: `compose_all_projections` now takes a `&Predictor`; `RecruitRow` SQL fetch extended with all freshman-model features (composite_rating, position_rank, previous_rank, height, weight, position, year, plus the school-context joins for committed-team prior AdjEM + per-(year, committed_team) AVG peer-class rating); per-class batch `predict_freshman_batch` runs once before bucketing, then per-recruit synthesis uses each row's prediction. `RecruitMeta.tier` now reflects the **model-projected tier** (was 247-rank-derived); UI tooltip in `Projected2027.tsx` updated to match. **Reassignment behaviour on class-of-2026 (N=367)**: T1 23/30 stable, T2 51/70 stable, T3 60/150 stable (with **47 T3→T2 promotions** — the model's strongest "school context overrules 101–250 rank" pattern), T4 102/117 stable. Duke's class is illustrative: Meyer (#110) moves T3→T2 because the model likes him at Duke (prior AdjEM + peer class), shifting Duke's tier breakdown from (3,1,1,0) to (3,2,0,0) without changing which recruits committed. **Honesty caveat**: 4-bucket discretisation loses some resolution at the extremes (predicted +12 and +6 both land in T1); per-stat multi-output regression is the future-proof fix and stays the principled-path follow-up.

  **Surface trajectory + freshman projections everywhere — 3-PR plan.** Driven by an observation (Q1 on regression-to-the-mean): the trajectory model systematically projects elite returners *lower* than their current-season CamPom — Boozer 2026 +30.54 → 2027 projected +15.93 (Δ −14.61). This is real signal (empirical regression-to-the-mean + selection bias on returners who stay + thin tail support in training data), but it'll surface as confusing negative Δs as soon as we put projected CamPom on Transfer and Projection surfaces. PR 1 lays the framing, PR 2 and PR 3 propagate the chip pattern from the Recruits tab to the rest of the site.
  - [x] **PR 1 — Honesty story for trajectory projections** — shipped. Added `mae_by_current_campom(df, oof)` diagnostic that buckets OOF kfold predictions by `prior_campom` (the model's input — what a player IS right now, not what they will be) and reports per-bucket MAE + mean predicted + mean actual + **bias**. Surfaces in `trajectory_model_meta.json::mae_by_current_campom` and prints inline at bottom of every retrain. Empirical results on the 4-class corpus (n=5,402 paired rows): bias is essentially zero across `[−5, +15]` (calibrated middle), positive on the `<−5` bust bucket (+0.40 — busts regress *up*), and **−3.42 on the +15..+20 bucket** (heavy regression). The **+20+ bucket is empty** — zero returners in training had current CamPom ≥+20, so Boozer-tier predictions are pure extrapolation beyond training distribution. `Predictor::predict_trajectory` docstring updated with the caveat + a pointer to ROADMAP §6 Q1. Tooltip on `PlayerDetail.tsx` and `PlayerProgression.tsx` gets a conditional regression note: `prior_campom ≥ 15` gets the full "model under-projects elite-tier returners by ≈−3 CamPom; read the q90 ceiling for the optimistic case" line; `10 ≤ prior_campom < 15` gets a brief "mild regression expected (~−0.3 bias)" note; below that the calibrated-zone tooltip is unchanged. Also fixed a stale "37-feature vector" reference in the Rust docstring (was 48 since the recruit-features PR).
  - [x] **PR 2 — Projected CamPom + Δ on Transfer page** — shipped. New `Predictor::predict_trajectory_batch(rows: &[[f32; TRAJECTORY_NUM_FEATURES]]) -> Result<Vec<TrajectoryPrediction>, ort::Error>` in `crates/cstat-core/src/inference.rs` mirrors `predict_freshman_batch` exactly (single contiguous `[N, 48]` tensor per ONNX session, 3 ONNX runs total regardless of N, empty-input short-circuit, output-length check). New `fetch_player_trajectory_rows(pool, &[Uuid], season)` helper in `crates/cstat-core/src/trajectory.rs` does a single batch SQL fetch keyed by `player_id = ANY($1)` (same join shape and qualification gate as `fetch_player_trajectory_row`, returns `HashMap<Uuid, TrajectoryPlayerRow>`). `/api/transfers/{year}` route extended with per-matched-transfer projection — one batch fetch + one batch predict, splice predictions back by enriched-row index. Failure semantics: any fetch or batch error logs once at warn and serves NULL projections route-wide. Frontend `web/src/components/TransferPortal.tsx` gains a `Proj` column (CamPom-tier-colored chip with q10–q90 band tooltip + regression-bias note when `current ≥ +10`) and a `ΔCP` column (`projected − current`, color-coded; negative=red is the regression-typical case for elite transfers, positive=green is the "model expects growth" case). Existing `Δ` column renamed to `Δ247` with tooltip updated to disambiguate from `ΔCP`. New `trajectory_batch_matches_single_row` test (with elite-tier sanity assertion that the +18 input regresses below current) + `trajectory_batch_empty_input` test in `inference.rs`. **Live coverage on /api/transfers/2026**: 1,299/1,497 transfers matched to cstat (87%), 1,177 of those passed the qual gate and got a projection (78% projection coverage of the full portal). Latency ~105 ms per request (release build, local Postgres). The top 2026 transfer cohort tops out at +15.35 CamPom — within the +10..+15 mild-regression bucket but below the +15+ heavy-regression zone, so `ΔCP` for top transfers is mostly near-flat (Bidunga +0.82, Blackwell +1.83, Miller +2.66, etc.).
  - [x] **PR 3 — Per-player projections on 2027 projection page** — shipped. Backend: `/api/projections/{year}/teams/{team_id}` route in `crates/cstat-api/src/routes/projections.rs` now batches **every** returner + portal arrival + uncertain (declared-draft) player through `fetch_player_trajectory_rows` + `predict_trajectory_batch` (the PR 2 shared infra) and splices per-player `projected_campom_{mean,lower,upper}` into the returning / arrivals / uncertain JSON cohorts. Recruits get the full band too: `compose_all_projections` in `crates/cstat-core/src/roster_projection.rs` switched from `Vec<Option<f64>>` (mean-only) to `Vec<Option<FreshmanPrediction>>` so the q10/q90 ride alongside the synthesized PlayerRow on `RecruitMeta.projected_campom_{lower,upper}`. `synthesize_freshman_row` continues to receive just the mean (it only needs that for tier reassignment). Uncertain rows now render a chip too ("if they withdraw and return, here's the projection") since they *are* returners under the ceiling scenario. Both `ProjectedReturning` (inherited by `ProjectedArrival`) and `ProjectedUncertain` carry the new fields in `web/src/api/client.ts`. The old separate `CamPom` + `Proj` columns merged into a single `Projection` column rendering `current → projected` with the projected chip as the visual headline (same pattern as the team-detail PlayerCard). `rank_cstat` is now the row's position when the 247-ranked transfer cohort is sorted by projected CamPom desc — propagating into `Δ247 = rank_247 − projected_rank` (was current-CamPom rank before). Sort comparator tiebreaks the no-projection cohort on source-season CamPom so those rows are at least internally sensible. Coverage impact on /api/transfers/2026: 323/330 ranked transfers retain a `rank_cstat` (loss of 7 = 2% — much smaller than the 25% conservative estimate before the live count). Top-5 reshuffle: Bidunga still #1, Allen Graves jumps over Momcilovic on projection despite slightly lower current CamPom. Recruits page was already projection-ranked from the freshman-impact PR — no change there beyond confirming alignment. **Live spot-check on Duke 2027**: returners all show modest positive Δs (Boozer +2.9, Ngongba +3.5, Sarr +3.2, Foster +0.4 — mid-tier returners on the growth side); arrivals near-flat (Blackwell +13.2 → +15.0); recruits show the freshman-model bands (Cameron Williams T1 +11.8 with band 3.2–19.0 = elite-tail uncertainty); **Cameron Boozer surfaces the canonical regression case** — current +30.5 → projected +15.8 (Δ −14.7) with the +15 heavy-regression tooltip note explaining why. **Skipped within scope**: "Top projected CamPom" summary column on the main projections grid; the team-detail per-player surface is the load-bearing one. Easy follow-up if it turns out to be needed.

  **Dependency sequencing now**:
  - Phase 5c growth model can take recruit-rank as a feature *immediately* — the `recruits` table is populated and joins via `cstat_player_id` (resolves automatically once cstat-season 2027 box scores ingest).
  - The freshman-impact *prior model* is no longer soft-blocked — 558 paired classes is enough for a v1 fit. Wider bands than ideal until more classes accumulate, but the heuristic-vs-prior comparison is now an A/B we can actually run.
  - 5c is the natural neighbor for "year 1 → year 2 onward"; the prior model covers "year 0 → year 1". Together they project any player's next-season line regardless of where they are in their career arc.
- [x] **Backtest models across multiple seasons** — DONE. Game models retrained across 12 seasons (#78, chronological backtest); the roster-impact projection has a 5-season (2022–2026) leave-one-season-out backtest (#87, #96, 1,326 team-seasons); the predict model has a leave-one-season-out game backtest (#92, pooled AUC 0.815). Per-season LOSO ONNX artifacts persisted under `training/models/loso/`.
- [x] **CamPom overfitting audit & point-in-time predict backtest** *(measurement phase shipped 2026-05-29; production wiring queued below)* — all three nested tests landed, full audit doc + infographic in `training/eval_history/honest_audit_findings_20260529.md` and `audit_final_comparison_20260529.png`. **Headline: honest predict AUC = 0.785 (vs. leaky 0.816), inflation = 0.031 AUC of intra-season lookahead.** cstat still beats NatStat ELO (0.722) by 0.063 AUC honestly.
  - [x] **Constants sensitivity sweep** — `training/campom_sensitivity_sweep.py`. **All five `CAMPOM_*` knobs robust under ±20% perturbation** (Pearson r ≥ 0.997 vs. baseline, top-50 overlap 47-49/50). CamPom constants are NOT overfit; signal comes from the underlying GBPM/usage/minutes structure, not precisely-tuned weights. Verdict closes the "tuning-overfit" prong of the audit.
  - [x] **Leave-one-season-out (LOSO) game-prediction backtest** — `training/train_loso.py`. Pooled AUC = **0.815** across 6 holdouts (2021-2026); per-season range 0.802-0.824. **Essentially identical to the leaky 5-fold OOF (0.816)** — the cross-season leak channel is ≈ 0. Leakage decomposition shows LOSO's coef on `leak_diff` = 3.35 (vs. 5-fold 3.46) — unchanged. The intra-season `torvik_player_stats.cam_gbpm_v3` aggregate is the entire leakage budget. Persisted: per-season models + OOF predictions in `training/models/loso/predict_{year}/`.
  - [x] **Point-in-time CamPom backtest** *(gold standard)* — full pipeline shipped:
    - Migration `022_torvik_player_game_stats.sql` (1.3M rows / 12 seasons backfilled via the new `cstat-ingest torvik --persist-games` flag — no new API calls; the gzip JSON was already being fetched for rebound backfill).
    - Python prototype `training/compute_campom_at.py` validated against season aggregate at **Pearson r = 0.92** (no-SOS variant; Rust port should hit >0.99 with conf-SOS).
    - `training/build_pit_lookup.py` precomputes a 14-day-cadence player×season×date grid (501k rows, 22 MB compressed).
    - `features.py` patched with `GBPM_VARIANT=pit_cam_v3` asof-merging the pit grid into the standard 49-feature matrix.
    - Retrained model in `models_experiments/pit_cam_v3/`: **AUC = 0.785 / MAE = 8.56 / ATS = 67.69% vs Vegas**. Leak coefficient cut in half (3.46 → 1.88); residual is honest signal via `diff_adj_efficiency_margin` correlating with team-improvement, not lookahead.
  - **Four independent methods triangulate honest AUC ≈ 0.785**: pit retrain (0.785), linear leak subtraction (0.786), March-tournament games (0.794), no-leak-bucket regression (0.760).
  - **Documentation**: full audit findings + recommendations in `training/eval_history/honest_audit_findings_20260529.md`. **Net publishable claim: cstat predict AUC is 0.785, not 0.816** — `docs/model_performance.md` needs to be updated as part of the production-promotion item below.
- [x] **Promote pit_cam_v3 to production (in-season usability)** *(shipped — the honest model is served on the `as_of_date` predict path; the one remaining freshness step, daily torvik-in-cron, is folded into "In-season pipeline hardening" Phase 6)* — the audit proved the honest model exists at AUC 0.785; this item is the engineering work to make it the production-served model. **Today's predict path uses end-of-season state for historical games (admitted in `TeamDetail.tsx:736`); the in-season production path doesn't exist yet because pit features are Python-only and aren't computed on demand.** Five sub-steps, sequenced:
  - [x] **Port `compute_campom_at(date)` to Rust** — landed as `cstat_core::pit_campom::compute_pit_campom` (separate module from the season-wide `compute_campom`, since materializing per-date is intentionally not a pipeline step). Aggregates from `torvik_player_game_stats` up to a cutoff and applies the v3 chain. Conf-SOS adjustment intentionally skipped to match the `pit_cam_v3` training variant — that's the input distribution the shipped model was trained on; adding SOS at serve time would reintroduce train/serve skew. Unit-tested against Python prototype outputs (`compute_one_matches_python_baseline` etc.).
  - **Daily `torvik --persist-games` in the cron** → **moved to "In-season pipeline hardening" (Phase 6)**, where the nightly cron lives. Folds the full-season `barttorvik.com/{year}_all_advgames.json.gz` fetch (one HTTP call, ~30s, no NatStat budget) into the nightly run so `torvik_player_game_stats` — the pit_cam_v3 serving input — stays fresh.

  - [x] **Wire the `/api/predict` route to use pit features when `as_of_date` is set** — `Predictor` gained `predict_pit{,_total,_with_contributions}`; `features::build_all_features_pit` substitutes the pit map for the leaky `torvik_player_stats.gbpm/ogbpm/dgbpm` channel; `/api/predict?as_of_date=YYYY-MM-DD` and `predict_projection` thread the cutoff end-to-end. **TeamDetail Projected column on completed games is now backed by pit predictions** (`game_date − 1` cutoff), closing the long-standing `TeamDetail.tsx:736` caveat — copy was strengthened to "Pre-game projection (point-in-time CamPom as of {date})" in the schedule cell tooltip. Predict page gained an "As of" date input; URL deep-links carry `as_of_date` from TeamDetail schedule click-throughs.

Benchmark + historical-context sections corrected (74.6% → honest 72.2%). Numbers pulled from `training/models_experiments/pit_cam_v3/model_meta.json` + `oof_predictions_ats_summary.json` (ATS 7,418–3,541–143 = 67.7%; the JSON's `win_pct` field is mis-computed, wins/losses give the right figure).
  - **Acceptance**: a `GET /api/predict?home=Duke&away=UNC&season=2027` call serves point-in-time features as of today; backfilled historical predictions use pit_cam_v3 at game_date − 1; `model_meta.json` carries `gbpm_variant = pit_cam_v3` and the published AUC is 0.785.
- [x] **Preseason × pit blend for honest early-season predictions** *(shipped — core 2026-05-30; crossover calibration 2026-06-05, #111. `preseason_blend_weight` decay + `prediction_basis` chip live in `routes/predict.rs`.)* — the audit's R5 wiring shipped, but spot-checks against real 2026 games exposed the next layer: very-early-season games where `torvik_player_game_stats` is essentially empty produce *correctly honest* but *useless* predictions (Duke vs Army 2025-11-11 honest-projects at +1.5; actual was +55, because on 2025-11-10 there were ~0 per-game Torvik rows for the season). Pit predictions converge to sharpness by mid-January (Duke vs Wake Forest 2026-01-24: pred +22.2 vs actual +21), but the first ~4 weeks of every season are the regime where honesty alone isn't enough — we need a prior to anchor on while the in-season signal accrues.
  > KenPom's published methodology blends preseason ratings with current-season performance, with preseason weight decaying as the season progresses until ~early January when preseason drops out entirely. cstat already has the preseason side built (the `/api/projections/{year}` "Future" tab from §5b Phase B); the missing pieces are (a) validating preseason quality so the blend isn't propagating sticky last-year noise, and (b) the time-weighted blend mechanic itself.

  **Three nested investigations**, sequenced by dependency. Don't skip (1) — blending bad preseason into an honest pit feed makes the in-season prediction *worse*.

  1. [x] **Preseason projection accuracy audit** *(shipped 2026-05-29 — `training/eval_history/preseason_audit_20260529.md`)*. Built `training/audit_preseason_projections.py` (consumes the new `cstat-ingest projections-backtest --output PATH` per-team JSON dump) — joins per-team predictions to DB-derived signals (returning-minutes share, portal in/out cam_v3, recruit class strength), runs per-actual-quartile bias decomposition, OLS residual regression, and a global slope test. **Three load-bearing findings**:
     - **Honesty is clean**: all three projection-pipeline components are leak-free at the season level for this backtest. The trajectory model's `leave_one_pair_out` (in `train_trajectory_model.py`) is misnamed — it actually holds out each unique `(s_n, s_np1)` pair, which IS leave-one-target-season-out at the pair level. Predictions persisted to `trajectory_oof_predictions` are those LOSO predictions. LOSO MAE 2.133 vs 5-fold MAE 2.130 — tight gap, no season-level memorization. Roster-impact (LOSO) and freshman (LOCO) were already known honest. **The 5.88 MAE is honest signal-to-noise, not leakage.** Initial concern that trajectory k-fold OOF would be the leakage channel was wrong — the function naming threw me. Doc in the trajectory training script could be sharper.
     - **Systematic per-actual-quartile bias** (the accuracy lever): Q1 bottom over-projected by +6.09 AdjEM on average, Q4 top under-projected by −4.35. Global slope test returns 1.05 (near 1) — the bias is invisible on a calibration plot and only surfaces when conditioned on actual. Pure linear de-shrinkage buys +0.05 MAE because most teams sit near center; the fix must come from better tail features, not a global rescale. By-returning-minutes-share is flat across quartiles (the "sticky last year" theory in the original §2 candidate list doesn't show up — that candidate is *dropped*).
     - **OLS-significant missing feature (n=1)**: `portal_outbound_cam_v3` (β std +0.978, t=+2.53, p<0.05). Teams losing high-cam_v3 talent in the portal are systematically over-projected — Phase B aggregates the projected cam_v3 of *arrivals + returners + recruits* but doesn't see the *cam_v3 of departures* explicitly. Maryland 2026 is the cleanest case: projected +25.6, actual +5.2, net portal −8. Recruit-related signals all directionally consistent (more recruits → under-projected = top programs over-perform) but not significant at p<0.05; suggests the freshman model's documented selection-bias for top recruits is the upstream cause.

  3. **In-season blend (KenPom-style decay schedule)** *(the user-visible payoff — **core shipped 2026-05-30**)*

     **✓ Shipped (output blend + date schedule v1).** End-to-end: **(a)** migration `023_team_preseason_projection.sql` + `cstat-ingest compute-projections --year` materializes each team's served projection AdjEM (floor/ceiling/midpoint) via the shared `cstat_core::roster_projection::score_projection_adj_em` (the exact fn `/api/projections` uses, so persisted == served; calibration consts `PROJECTION_SHRINK_WEIGHT`/`PROJECTION_OFFSET`/`MIN_QUALIFYING_FOR_PROJECTION` centralized in cstat-core, the route now aliases them). Populated 2024–2026 (281/263/232 teams; the rest too-thin → fall back to pit). Keyed by target-season UUID (base→target resolved via `natstat_id`). **(b)** `/api/predict` applies the output blend on the honest pit path (`as_of_date` set): `blended_margin = w(D)·preseason_margin + (1−w)·pit_margin`, `preseason_margin = projected_adjem_home − projected_adjem_away + HCA(venue)`, win prob re-derived from the blended margin, **totals stay pit** (preseason has no totals — deferred). Scalar blend of already-venue-resolved margins → neutral symmetry + away-flip preserved exactly (verified: Duke/Florida neutral +5.00/−5.00, winp sums to 1.0). Graceful fallback to pit when either team lacks a projection row. **(c)** `prediction_basis` extended to `preseason`|`blended`|`pit`|`leaky`; Predict-page chip color-codes the active regime. **(d)** the shared `predict_projection` helper (consumed by `routes/teams.rs` TeamDetail Projected column + `routes/ticker.rs`) applies the same blend, so a played early-season game's projected score on TeamDetail agrees with the Predict page (verified: Duke vs Army 2025-11-11 TeamDetail proj +45.9 — Duke-as-visitor-at-Army venue — vs the old pit-only ~+1.5). **Result:** Duke vs Army 2025-11-10 now predicts **+53.3 (blended)** vs the old useless +1.5 (actual was +55), decaying to pure pit (+38.4) by late January. All cargo check/fmt/clippy/test (13 api + 7 core) + tsc green.

     **Crossover calibration — SHIPPED 2026-06-05 (`cstat-ingest measure-blend-accuracy --years 2024,2025,2026`).** The subcommand replays each season's games through honest point-in-time inference (`build_all_features_pit` as of `game_date − 1`), pools the shared subset across seasons, and grid-searches the schedule shape (peak weight × decay-end day × HCA) against blended-margin MAE. **Result (pooled, n=3,475):** the old v1 curve (w=1.0, Nov 1 → Jan 15 = 75 days, HCA 3.5) scored MAE **9.01**; the optimum is **peak weight 0.70, linear decay to 0 over 42 days (≈ Dec 13), HCA 3.5** → MAE **8.80**, within 0.03 of the per-week oracle ceiling (8.77) and beating pit-only (9.15). Two findings: pit overtakes preseason ~2 weeks in (not mid-January — the old endpoint kept weight on a stale prior for a month), and the opening blend is best at 0.70/0.30 (the two imperfect legs ensemble; opening-week blended 9.84 vs preseason-only 10.91). Stable across all three seasons (beats v1 in each). Applied to `predict.rs` (`PRESEASON_PEAK_WEIGHT = 0.70`, `PRESEASON_DECAY_DAYS = 42`; HCA unchanged at 3.5); `prediction_basis` labels the dominant leg (`preseason` while w ≥ 0.5, else `blended`, then `pit`). Re-tune by re-running the subcommand. *Caveats: early-season "neutral" labels in `games` are imperfect, so the HCA leg is directional; the schedule is linear-from-open (the oracle row bounds the residual headroom from a fancier shape).*

     **Live-path blend — SHIPPED 2026-07-15.** Until now the blend fired **only when the request carried `as_of_date`** — so the live serving surfaces (default Predict page, `predict_projection` → TeamDetail Projected column + ScoreTicker) served the un-anchored current-state model over a 1–2 game sample on opening night, exactly the regime the blend exists to fix. Both blend sites now compute the weight from `as_of_date.unwrap_or_else(today_utc)`, so a **live** request blends too. Guardrails: the live weight comes from `live_blend_weight`, which returns 0 before the season's Nov 1 open (a pre-open live request — e.g. browsing next season in October — must NOT blend against a degenerate empty-season margin; explicit-`as_of_date` probing is still allowed pre-open, floor-guarded to Sep 1). Win-prob σ is **per-bundle**: pit σ on the `as_of_date` path (leg is the honest pit model), prod σ on the live path (leg is the prod/leaky model) — keeps each path self-consistent and continuous across the ~Dec-13 decay-off boundary. The blend logic is unified in one `apply_preseason_blend` helper (`routes/predict.rs`) shared by both sites. `prediction_basis`/chip unchanged (`preseason`/`blended` when engaged). Because it's off-season now, the live blend is a no-op until Nov 2026 and can only be exercised via the M4 `simulate` harness (or `CSTAT_SIMULATED_DATE`) until then. Hardened over three code-review rounds.

     - **Blend mechanic**: at game date D in season Y, define a weight `w(D) ∈ [0, 1]` that schedules from 1.0 in October to 0.0 by ~mid-January. Two implementation options:
       - **Team-feature blend** (cleaner): `team_feature_i = w(D) × preseason_feature_i + (1 − w(D)) × pit_feature_i`. Requires the preseason output (currently `/api/projections/{year}` returning AdjEM only) to expose the full 49-feature roster aggregate so each component can be blended. Bigger lift on the preseason side, but the result is a single set of features fed through one model.
       - **Output blend** (simpler): predict twice (once preseason-fed, once pit-fed), blend the *output margins* by `w(D)`. No new feature plumbing, but the win-prob and totals blend then need their own calibration. Likely the right starting point.
     - **Weight schedule**: piecewise linear is a reasonable v1 — `w = 1.0` before Nov 1, linear decay to `w = 0` by Jan 15. Calibrate the dates against a per-day rolling MAE (preseason-only vs pit-only) on 2024 + 2025 + 2026 historical games — the crossover point is where current-season signal beats preseason, and that's where `w → 0` should land.
     - **Alternative weighting** — instead of date, schedule on *games played* (`w = 1` at GP=0, `w = 0` at GP=15, linear in between). More principled because the underlying decision is "how much in-season data do we have to anchor on," not "what month is it." Worth backtesting both.
     - **API contract**: add `prediction_basis: "preseason" | "blended" | "pit"` to the `/api/predict` response so the frontend can render appropriate framing ("preseason projection" vs "in-season honest" vs "blended"). Frontend chip: small label next to the headline margin showing which regime is active.
     - **Edge case — opening week**: even with the blend, the very first game has GP=0 for both teams, so the prediction is pure preseason. That's fine — frame it that way. The Army-Duke example becomes "preseason: Duke −15" rather than "honest: Duke −1.5."
     - **Backtest acceptance**: opening-3-weeks MAE drops from the current honest-pit MAE (need to measure — likely 18-20+ given the Army case) to something close to the Phase B preseason MAE (5.86). Mid-season MAE stays near 8.28. The crossover region (mid-Dec to mid-Jan) is the load-bearing measurement.
     - **Deferred**: also blend for the totals model and TreeSHAP attributions — both follow from getting margin/win blending right.

  **Acceptance for the whole bullet**: opening-week predictions read sensibly (Duke −15 on 2025-11-11 vs Army, not −1.5); the chip on the Predict page tells the user which regime is active; a `cstat-ingest measure-blend-accuracy --year YYYY` subcommand produces a per-week MAE table that validates the chosen `w(D)` schedule. Out-of-scope here: any product surfacing of the preseason-only "Future" tab in the in-season window — that's a §5b polish, not predict-route work.

- [ ] **Tighten the residual pit-retrain leak** *(low priority, opportunistic)* — the pit_cam_v3 retrain has a residual leak coef of 1.88 (vs 3.46 in leaky baseline). About half of that is `diff_adj_efficiency_margin` correlating with team-improvement (legitimate signal), but ~14-day cadence in the pit lookup grid means up to 13 days of stale CamPom contributes to some real residual leakage. Two cheap wins, only worth doing if calibration on early-season games disappoints:
  - **Reduce pit lookup cadence from 14d to 1d during the season** — would give exact pre-game CamPom rather than "what we knew up to 2 weeks ago." Grows the pit lookup from 501k rows to ~6.5M rows; still well under 1 GB.
  - **Quantify per-feature contribution to the residual leak coef** by holding `pit_diff` fixed and regressing `leak_diff` on each non-CamPom feature's contribution. If `diff_adj_efficiency_margin` explains most of 1.88, we're done. If not, hunt the remaining feature(s) for analogous point-in-time fixes.
- [x] **ATS backtest harness** *(measurement-grade shipped 2026-05-29; LOSO-wired version queued below)* — Python harness `training/eval_ats.py` consumes any OOF CSV (or directory of LOSO OOFs), joins to `game_forecasts`, computes ATS win/loss/push + ROI at -110 vig with edge-bucketed reporting. Used to validate the audit findings: **leaky 5-fold OOF = 71.81% ATS / +37.10% ROI; LOSO = 72.22% / +37.87% (cross-season holdout unchanged); pit_cam_v3 honest = 67.69% / +29.22%.** The edge-bucket signature confirms the smoke test was lookahead (|edge|≥8 bucket: leaky 92.0%, LOSO 92.0%, honest 91.7% — most blowouts agree regardless of features; the middle buckets are where leakage was inflating the picks). Sample-of-record snapshotted to `eval_history/ats_smoke_20260529.json`.
  - **CLI shape** *(new `cstat-ingest backtest-ats` subcommand)*: takes `--seasons`, `--sample N`, optional `--model-dir` override, optional `--asof-mode {endseason,pointintime}`. Pulls games from `game_forecasts` with non-null `spread` + both `home_moneyline` + `away_moneyline` + final score; runs each through the chosen model; writes a per-game result JSONL (`game_id`, `predicted_margin`, `abs_spread`, `fav_side` from more-negative ML, `bet_side`, `outcome`, `edge`) plus a summary table. Default `--asof-mode endseason` matches today's smoke-test behavior; `pointintime` unlocks once `compute_campom_at(date)` lands. Lives in `crates/cstat-ingest/src/backtest.rs` so it can call the live predictor in-process (no HTTP round-trip — the 400-game smoke test took 8s via HTTP; bumping to 15k full-coverage games at ~20ms in-process is ~5 min, viable for CI).
  - **Edge-bucketed reporting** *(load-bearing)*: report ATS win% + ROI bucketed by `|cstat_margin − abs_spread|` at `<1 / <3 / <5 / <8 / ≥8`. A real edge shows a roughly flat curve peaking in the mid buckets; a leakage-inflated model shows the smoke-test signature (monotonic ramp from ~55% → 95%). The shape of the curve is more diagnostic than the headline number. Same buckets, separately, for moneyline-implied vs cstat win-probability — pairs with the §"Model accuracy dashboard with calibration tracking" item to give a two-eyed view (margin via ATS, prob via Brier / ECE / log loss vs market).
  - **Cross-validation harness wiring** *(the unlock)*: once LOSO model artifacts exist from the overfitting-audit item above (`models/game_predict_loso/game_predict_{year}.onnx`), `backtest-ats` should pick the right held-out model per game so the in-season backtest is leak-free. Without this, every "honest" ATS run requires manual model-juggling. Mirror the trajectory model's per-season LOSO loader (`inference::TrajectoryModel`).
  - **Comparison surfaces**: alongside cstat, include baseline columns for **NatStat ELO** (`home_win_exp` against ML-implied prob, treated as a margin proxy via `spread = Φ⁻¹(p) × 11`) and **flat-favorite** ("always bet favorite") + **flat-underdog** sanity-check baselines. Calibrated baselines make it obvious whether cstat's number is real signal or just "the market is bad on this slice."
  - **Stretch — Kelly sizing report**: convert each bet's `edge` + market odds into a fractional-Kelly stake (cap at 1% of bankroll for sanity), compute compounded bankroll growth across the test window. Don't ship as a recommendation engine — ship as a "what if you'd bet flat-stake vs Kelly" comparison so the variance story is visible alongside the headline ROI. Easy to add once per-bet result rows exist.
  - **Sample-of-record**: snapshot the 400-game smoke-test result (`/tmp/ats_results.json`) into `training/eval_history/ats_smoke_20260529.json` before that tmp file disappears. The delta between this end-of-season-state run and the eventual point-in-time run is the "leakage budget" measurement — load-bearing for any future "is the audit working?" check.
  - **Acceptance**: the harness is honest infrastructure once it (a) runs cleanly against LOSO models, (b) sources CamPom point-in-time, (c) is consumed by the calibration dashboard, and (d) the |edge|≥8 bucket has collapsed from ~95% toward the 50–60% range. **Anti-acceptance**: do not surface ATS numbers publicly on the site under any framing — internal measurement only. The site is a college basketball analytics platform, not a betting tool, and `campom.org` should not become indexable as the latter.

What's missing is the production wrapping: a nightly scheduler, idempotency guarantees, failure detection, and a test plan that exercises the live path *now* (we can't afford to discover a regression on opening night). The whole live prediction/projection product rides on this. **→ M1 (complete + correct nightly) SHIPPED 2026-06-29:** new `cstat-ingest nightly` orchestrator (`SeasonIngester::nightly`) folds **Torvik `--persist-games` + forecasts + `/elo` ratings in BEFORE compute** — fixing two latent staleness bugs a plain nightly `update` would have: (a) rebuilding `cam_gbpm_v3` / `pit_cam_v3` from *stale* Torvik, and (b) never refreshing `team_season_stats.elo_rating` (the served `diff_elo_rating` feature, whose sole writer is the `/elo` step) — plus the `ingest_runs` per-step ledger (migration 039, `run_ledger.rs`, excluded from `sync_to_prod`). Verified live (forecasts + Torvik + ledger steps). **→ M2 (scheduled + observable) code SHIPPED 2026-06-29:** `GET /api/health/ingest` (per-step freshness from `ingest_runs`, 503 when a served-critical step is >36h stale), fail-soft per-channel Slack notifications (`crates/cstat-ingest/src/notify.rs` `SlackChannel` registry, `SLACK_WEBHOOK_CRON` → `#cron-job-alerts` — success heartbeat / degraded / critical, one post per run), rate-budget headroom logging (warn on low remaining), optional Cloudflare edge purge after compute (`CF_ZONE_ID`/`CF_CACHE_PURGE_TOKEN`), and `scripts/sync_to_prod.sh --tables` for the Railway-direct split. Only the Railway dashboard cron wiring is operator-side — runbook in `docs/deploy_nightly_cron.md`. **→ DEPLOYED + first prod nightly HEALTHY 2026-06-30:** the Railway cron service (own `railway.cron.json`: `cstat-ingest nightly`, `30 9 * * *`, no healthcheck) runs on schedule and posts a green Slack heartbeat end-to-end (all 8 ledger steps `ok`, `/api/health/ingest` healthy). Going live surfaced — and fixed — a latency landmine the localhost DB had masked: the ingest's **per-row DB loops** (forecasts ~28k queries, torvik per-game persist ~113k, rebound backfill ~113k UPDATEs) ran in seconds locally but took *tens of minutes to hours* over a cross-region/public-proxy prod DB connection. Fix: run the cron on the **private same-region DB URL** + batch all three loops (forecasts #158, torvik-persist #159, rebound-backfill batched via `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)`) — ~28k/113k queries → ~10-115 each. Nightly is now ~3-4 min. Full write-up in the plan doc's *"Production lesson — DB connection latency vs. per-row loops."* Slack success/degraded posts also reformatted to multi-line mrkdwn. **Remaining milestones M3–M5 are the first sub-items below**, followed by operator wiring. The runtime decision is **Railway-direct serving nightly + targeted `sync_to_prod --tables` for heavy local jobs** (see doc). The detailed sub-bullets after them (Scheduling, Daily torvik, Idempotency, Rate-limit, Failure detection, Edge cases, Out-of-season test plan, First-day checklist) are the original M1 brainstorm — mostly shipped in M1/M2; the still-live pieces fold into M3–M5.

Acceptance met. — make every external feed degrade gracefully so one dead dependency can't kill the run. **(a)** explicit HTTP timeouts on every client (`client.rs`, `torvik.rs`, `tfs.rs`, `tfs_recruits.rs` — today they inherit reqwest's effectively-infinite default; a stalled socket hangs the nightly). **(b)** a `cstat-ingest preflight`/`healthz` command that pings each feed (NatStat known-season fetch, Torvik, 247 page-1 if a JWT is present, DB) and reports reachable/auth-valid/stale; the orchestrator runs it first and skips+warns on a dead feed rather than hard-failing. **(c)** 247-JWT expiry handling — the #1 connectivity risk (~6h token, no renewal, hard-fails 401): decode the `exp` claim (or peek page 1), and on expiry skip the 247 step with a loud warning while keeping the last `data/transfers/{year}_raw.json` snapshot (stale-but-present beats empty) + a recapture runbook. **(d)** surface the silent NatStat v4→v3 fallback as a structured warning + a per-run flag in `ingest_runs` so a prolonged v4 outage is visible. **(e)** Torvik CSV schema guard — a header/column-count assertion that fails loudly instead of writing misaligned garbage if Bart inserts a column. *Acceptance: revoke the JWT / block Torvik / 500 from NatStat v4 → clear preflight skip + alert, serving-critical chain still completes.*

Mitigations shipped: a warn-once in `today_utc`, boot warnings in both the API server and the ingest CLI, and the nightly **marks the run degraded** (Slack line) when the var is set — but the safest posture is simply never setting it on a real service (use it only for local out-of-season testing; the `simulate` harness sets the clock programmatically, not via env). All three warning sites share `env_simulated_date()` — the exact parse `today_utc` uses — so an empty/unparsable value the clock ignores can't false-alarm. **(b) SHIPPED 2026-07-13** — `cstat-ingest simulate --year --from --to [--step-days N] [--reset] [--no-compute]` (`crates/cstat-ingest/src/simulate.rs`) replays a CSV-bootstrapped season through the **real** `SeasonIngester::nightly` against an **isolated sim DB** (docker-compose `postgres-sim` behind a `sim` profile, :5433, own volume; the command hard-refuses any URL resolving to `DATABASE_URL`/`PROD_DATABASE_URL`'s host/port/db). Offline mechanism: NatStat-v4-shaped pages synthesized from `data/natstat_csv/{year}` are seeded into the sim `api_cache` under the exact `(endpoint, range, offset)` keys the nightly computes (incl. the terminal empty page pagination needs); `/forecasts`+`/elo` seed as empty-success pages (exercising that edge case every window); a bogus API key makes fixture gaps fail loudly; Slack/Cloudflare/heartbeat are muted process-wide (`notify::set_suppressed`). Each window runs the new `cstat_core::invariants::check_season` gates (AdjEM holes, missing team_game_stats sides, W-L drift, both swapped-game detectors — the module M5's nightly gates will reuse), and a final idempotency re-run asserts zero row-count drift; any failure exits non-zero. Runs green in CI's Postgres service on every push (not `#[ignore]`d); skips cleanly when `DATABASE_URL` is unset so a no-DB `cargo test` still passes; never touches the configured DB (own `CREATE DATABASE`/`DROP … WITH (FORCE)`), so it's safe against a dev's real local DB. Deliberately offline — drives only the box-score ingest + compute (not full `nightly`), so no live NatStat/Torvik/ELO/forecasts network. The seeding + ingest code paths are shared with the `simulate` harness (`seed_window_objects` / `ingest_box_score_window` / `table_counts`, `#[doc(hidden)] pub`) so the two can't drift. **(d) SHIPPED 2026-07-16** — three edge-case smokes added to `ingest_replay.rs` (each in its own throwaway DB via a shared `IsolatedDb` helper, so the parallel test runner can't collide): postponed→final re-ingest overwrites the NULL score without duplicating the row; a cancelled game (no statlines) writes zero `team_game_stats` and trips no invariant; a conference realignment (`UPDATE teams.conference` + recompute) flips `is_conference`. **M4 complete.** *Continue running `simulate --reset` weekly from now to tipoff.*

  - [ ] **Dead-man's-switch heartbeat** *(future / optional — deferred 2026-07-12)* — `notify::ping_heartbeat()` already fires at the end of each nightly (base URL on success, `…/fail` on degrade) and is a no-op until `HEARTBEAT_URL` is set on the cron service. Setting it up means creating a heartbeat check on an external monitor (healthchecks.io / Cronitor / Better Stack) that pages when the daily ping goes missing — the one failure mode the in-run Slack alerts structurally can't cover (a run that never starts). **Partially redundant with (a)** above: pointing an uptime monitor at `/api/health/ingest` also catches a dead cron (at the 36h staleness mark), so pick whichever external mechanism you prefer — the heartbeat is faster/simpler, `/api/health/ingest` is richer but needs its own poller. Chose to defer for now; revisit if a skipped run ever slips through.
  - **Scheduling**: nightly cron at ~04:30 ET (after NatStat's ~3 AM ET nightly re-tabulation completes — `/stats` re-runs then, per `docs/natstat-api-v4.md`). Run `cstat-ingest update --from <yesterday> --to <today>` so late stat corrections from the prior day's window get picked up. Host options: Railway scheduled job, GitHub Actions scheduled workflow, or systemd timer on the deploy host. Pick whichever matches the prod-runtime story.
  - **Daily `torvik --persist-games`** (folded in from the pit_cam_v3 promotion): the nightly run must also refresh `torvik_player_game_stats` via the full-season fetch (`barttorvik.com/{year}_all_advgames.json.gz` — one HTTP call, ~30s, no NatStat budget impact). This is what keeps the point-in-time CamPom serving path (`pit_cam_v3`, the honest in-season predictor) fed during the season — without it the in-season predictions go stale even if NatStat games ingest fine.
  - **Idempotency**: `update --from X --to Y` must be safe to re-run on the same window without duplicating rows or corrupting derived stats. Most upsert paths in `games.rs` already use `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE`; audit the path end-to-end and add a regression test (run twice, assert row counts and key column values are identical).
  - **Rate-limit headroom**: the 500/hr cap is the hard ceiling. Peak Saturdays in March can hit 200+ games → 200 game-detail calls + perfs/teamperfs pagination. Worst-case daily budget consumption is ~40–60% — fine, but we should log per-run "tokens consumed" + emit an alert if a run uses >80% of the daily budget.
  - **Failure detection**: today, a failed run silently leaves stale data. Need (a) per-step success markers persisted to a `ingest_runs` table (start_at, end_at, status, rows_touched, api_calls), (b) a `/api/health/ingest` route that returns the timestamp of the last successful run per data source, (c) an alerting hook (Slack webhook, email, or just an exit-code-based job-failure email from the scheduler) when last-success is >36h old during season.
  - **Late stat corrections**: NatStat retabulates at 3 AM ET nightly. A game played on day N often has its final box score corrected on the day-N+1 retabulation. The default `update --from <yesterday> --to <today>` window catches this. Document the policy: derived stats (CamPom, percentiles, AdjEM) recompute on every nightly run, so a Tuesday correction silently propagates into Wednesday morning's rankings without manual intervention.
  - **Edge cases to exercise**:
    - Postponed game → ingested with `GameStatus != Final`, then corrected later. Verify the upsert overwrites scores when status flips.
    - Game cancelled outright → does anything write a phantom 0-0 row? (Probably not, but worth a smoke test.)
    - Player adds (mid-season transfer eligibility) → box-score path auto-creates the player row; verify no FK violations.
    - Conference re-classification (rare mid-season) → `is_conference` recompute should pick up the change on next compute pass.
    - ELO endpoint may be empty if NatStat hasn't run their nightly ELO update yet — `update` should not fail the whole run on a missing `/elo` payload.
    - Forecasts endpoint goes empty after a season ends — current code probably handles it (forecasts only exist for future games), but worth a check on March 14 vs March 16.
  - **Out-of-season test plan** (the load-bearing part — needs to be exercised before October 2026):
    - **Approach A (replay)**: Pick a recent fully-ingested window (e.g. 2026-02-01 to 2026-02-07). Snapshot the affected rows. Delete them. Run `cstat-ingest update --from 2026-02-01 --to 2026-02-07`. Diff the result against the snapshot. Repeat for several different-shaped windows (single-game day, 100-game Saturday, postponement day, conference-tournament day).
    - **Approach B (staging)**: Stand up a staging DB on Railway, point a separate `cstat-ingest` instance at it via the scheduler, run the nightly job out-of-season against the empty payload window. Confirm the job completes with zero-game-day handling. Won't test correctness, but catches scheduler/auth/connectivity regressions.
    - **Approach C (synthetic)**: Mock NatStat's API at the HTTP layer for integration tests — fixed payload fixtures for each endpoint, verified by replaying through the existing ingest path. Lives in `crates/cstat-ingest/tests/` and runs in CI on every push. Highest engineering cost but the only one that catches API-shape regressions without burning rate budget.
    - **Recommended**: Approach A for the immediate confidence check (run once a week between now and October), Approach C for ongoing CI protection, Approach B as a stretch goal if staging infra is cheap.
  - **First-day-of-season checklist** (run manually the morning of 2026-11-03 or whenever tipoff lands): (a) confirm scheduled job ran overnight, (b) check `/api/health/ingest` shows recent timestamps, (c) inspect a freshly-ingested box score on the frontend, (d) verify ML predict route returns plausible margins for opening-night games (might need a `season=2027` fallback during the first ~2 weeks when game volume is too thin for adj efficiency to converge — already partially handled but worth a spot check).
