    - **P-onfloor-2 — historical backfill — 2026 DONE (2026-06-08); approach simplified.** Used sub-option (a): re-fetch raw plays *with* onfloor via the **date-path** `playbyplay/{date}` stream (which carries `game.onfloorhome`/`onfloorvis`), NOT the per-game hydrate — `scripts/onfloor_backfill.sh [SEASON]` walks the season day-by-day (the date filter composes with pagination, so one day per fetch stays under `MAX_PAGES`), is idempotent/resumable (skips dates already ≥90% onfloor), and ends with `compute`. 2026 is now **98.5% onfloor-covered** (replaced ~86% SUB-replay). Cost was far below the 11-hr estimate: most pages were `api_cache` hits (the raw JSON was already banked from the original ingest; the re-fetch just re-parses it to extract onfloor), so a full season ran in ~1.5 hrs. *(Minor: the script's final log line had a bash-3.2 `$SEASON…`-ellipsis unbound-var bug — fixed to `${SEASON}`; it crashed after the fetch completed but before `compute`, which was then run manually.)*
    - **Pre-2026 onfloor coverage — RESOLVED: NONE, and non-backfillable (verified 2026-06-08).** The `playbyplay/{date}` date-path returns `NO_DATA` for every pre-2025-26 date tested (2024-12-07, 2025-02-15, 2025-03-23, 2025-04-05) — the API retains date-path PBP for only ~the current season. **Onfloor cannot be backfilled for any past season**; the corpus only grows prospectively via the nightly in-season capture (so that cron is now essential, not optional — a skipped season loses its onfloor forever). **However**, the **per-game hydrate** `games;playbyplay,lineups/mbb/{gamecode}` retains a server-computed **`lineups` object for ALL seasons** (verified 2015/2018/2020/2022/2025; 46–77 5-man units/game with `possessions`/`oppp`/`dppp`/`plusminus` + full O/D/margin box splits) — this is the ROADMAP's option (b) and the *correct* cross-season-consistent, exact lineup source (train↔serve same source, no replay/onfloor skew). It does NOT carry per-play onfloor (no shot-context) and is name-keyed (resolve to UUIDs). **Full strategy in `docs/pbp_utilization_scope.md`.** This redirects the planned "historical onfloor backfill" to a **`lineups`-object backfill** (new ingest path to build first; ~130 hrs API over 12 seasons).
    - **P-onfloor-2b — onfloor is per-play INCOMPLETE; needs union reconstruction (discovered & SHIPPED 2026-06-08).** The backfill surfaced that NatStat's `onfloorhome`/`onfloorvis`, though populated on ~99% of plays, list only **four** of the five players on ~45% of plays (and three on ~8%) — verified on the raw 2026 `play_by_play`. The original `stints_from_onfloor_rows` keyed a stint on the exact per-play set, so it shattered real stints into 4-man micro-fragments that the 5-man rollup discarded: **5-man lineup-time coverage was only ~45%**, which halved served lineup minutes ("top-5 lineups look too small") and gutted the bench/OFF sample (Darius Acuff 2026: stored OFF = 1 possession, on/off swing −201). **Fix** (`pbp_replay.rs`, unit-tested): UNION the per-play sets within a run of plays (a true stint's union converges to exactly five) and treat the first play whose union would exceed five as the substitution boundary. **Impact on 2026**: 5-man coverage **45% → 85%**, stint rows **1.66M → 615k**, per-player on-floor minutes corrected (Acuff 17.9 → 31.2 min/game), and the on/off swing distribution went from garbage to sane (rotation players ≥200 OFF poss: p05/p50/p95 = −29.5/−0.6/+21.4; 92% of rows now carry ≥100 OFF possessions). Full writeup in `docs/pbp_methodology.md` ("On-floor union reconstruction"). **Two follow-on fixes SHIPPED 2026-06-08** for the iron-man thin-OFF residual: (i) **0-minute clamp guard** — the lineup box-minute clamp now treats a member's 0/NULL box minutes as *unknown* (`pgs.minutes > 0`) instead of collapsing the ceiling to 1.0, fixing the ~20%-of-clamp-killed-minutes false positives from `starter=t`/0-min artifacts (~1.1k player-games); 5-man valid coverage 71.4% → 72.5% of floor-time. (ii) **on/off rewritten to read ALL stints + a per-player box-minute cap** — on/off attributes by individual presence, so it no longer needs a clean 5-man unit or the lineup clamp (recovering the bench windows lost when ~19% of onfloor codes don't resolve to a roster row), and it caps each player's per-game ON at his box minutes (scale-down only) to undo the reconstruction's ON over-credit, flowing the excess to OFF. A **minimum-ON-sample gate (≥100 on-court possessions)** also replaced the old `>0` gate — reading all stints gave benchwarmers a tiny noisy ON slice (±600/100 ratings) that topped the swing rankings; 100 poss matches the panel's OFF threshold, drops only sub-~3-min/game players, and cut 2026 extreme-swing rows (|swing|>60) from 211 to 1. **Result: Acuff OFF 11 → 271 poss, swing −201 → −13; healthy-OFF rows 92% → 96%; ON over-credit (>2 min/g vs box) down to 2%; on/off swing dist p01/p50/p99 = −29/−1.3/+23 over 3,969 rotation players.** Reconciliation + parity tests green. **Remaining residual**: an iron-man's OFF *rate* (not amount) is still an estimate — the moved possessions are scaled at his on-court rate, not measured from the sparse-onfloor bench windows. Principled end-state remains **RAPM** (below).
    - **P-onfloor-3 — serving + unlocks**: once onfloor lineups exist, the `source` flag flips to `onfloor` (already surfaced in the UI tooltip), the box-minute clamp becomes a no-op for those games, and the **defensive lineup ratings** + play-style fingerprints (`ptspaint`/`ptsbrk`/`pts2ch`/`ptsoffto` and their `-d` defensive counterparts) become available to the team-lineups surface and the on/off / lineup-quality features. The replay oracle test (`pbp_replay_oracle`) already measures replay-vs-onfloor agreement, so we can quantify exactly how much the upgrade buys per season before committing the API budget. **NOTE (2026-06-08)**: the clamp's 0-minute false positives are now fixed (P-onfloor-2b), and on/off no longer depends on the lineup clamp at all (reads all stints + per-player box cap). 2026 on/off + lineup_aggregates are recomputed locally with all the above and are **ready to sync to prod** (`./scripts/sync_to_prod.sh`) — pending a sign-off; prod also needs migrations `034`/`035` on the next API deploy.
    - **P-onfloor-4 — PBP utilization scope, the lineup-source decision, and the Tier-1 KEY-RULE verification (scoped 2026-06-08/09; full strategy in `docs/pbp_utilization_scope.md`).** Stepping back from the in-the-weeds onfloor fixes to settle *what we build on top of PBP and in what order*, under the governing constraint: **anything we train a served model on must be computable in-season from the nightly cron API fetch, with no train/serve skew.** Three tiers:
      - **Tier 1 — skew-free tag features** (paint/perimeter mix, transition, 2nd-chance, points-off-TO, fouls-drawn, A/TO): **direct `player_id` tag rollups**, *independent of any lineup source*, already prod-resident in `player_game_stats`. **← CLOSED 2026-06-10: DB + web UI shipped; the models pass was backtested and rejected in all three families — Tier-1 is serving-only (plan + verdicts at the bottom of this item).** **Coverage correction (found 2026-06-09 during the models pass): contextual tags exist only 2020+.** The 2015–2018 feeds carry *only* box-event tags (`FGA`/`FGM`/`REB`/`TOV`/…) — zero `paint`/`brk`/`2ch`/`offto`/`FOULED` vocabulary (paint share of tagged FGA: 0.000 for 2015–2018 vs ≥0.41 for every 2020+ season) — so the earlier "all seasons 2015–2026" claim was wrong; deriving from those vintages yields misleading zeros (every shot "perimeter", 0 transition pts, 0 fouls drawn), which is exactly what had been served on the 2015–2018 player PBP panels. **Fixed**: `compute_pbp_aggregates` now has a second gate, `pbp_lacks_context_tags` (paint-share-of-FGA < 0.05 → clear + skip the tag-derived aggregates; lineups/possessions unaffected since box-event tags + SUBs exist in all vintages), and the 2015–2018 per-game columns / season rates / percentiles were NULL-cleared locally. Effective Tier-1 feature coverage: **2020–2026 (7 usable seasons)** — 2019 was already excluded by the corrupt gate, and 2015–2018 are now excluded by this one.
      - **Tier 2 — lineup-membership features** (on/off, `lineup_quality`, partner combos): depend on *who shared the floor*; gated on the source decision below.
      - **Tier 3 — RAPM** (the adjusted-+/- item below): soft-blocked on Tier 2's exact lineups.
      - **The lineup-membership source decision.** Three sources, by fidelity: **`replay`** (~86%, all seasons, SUB-replay off the CSV subs) / **`onfloor`** (exact per-play *with opponent pairing*, 2026-only, **non-backfillable**) / the **`lineups` object** (exact, per-game-aggregated 5-man units with possessions/oppp/dppp/plusminus, **no opponent pairing, no per-play**, available for **all seasons** via the per-game hydrate *and* in-season nightly). **Decision: the `lineups` object becomes the canonical membership source for the served aggregates (`lineup_aggregates`, `player_on_off`) and site display across *all* seasons including 2026** — it's exact, and it's the *same source train↔serve* (collapsing the replay-history / onfloor-2026 skew into one), which is what the KEY RULE demands. `replay` is demoted to a per-game fallback; **`onfloor` is RETAINED, not discarded** — it's strictly richer *per-play* (shot-context + the opponent-5 pairing RAPM needs), so it stays the per-play corpus and the prospective 2027+ capture. *This is not "migrate everything to lineups": tags are orthogonal (not a lineup source), and onfloor stays for the per-play tier.* **Gated on a name-resolution de-risk** — the object keys units by abbreviated name (`"D. Swain · M. Foster · …"`), resolved to UUIDs against the game's box rosters (reusing the null-sub initial+lastname fallback in `compute_pbp_lineups`); within-roster collisions are the failure mode. If the de-risk fails, the decision reverts to `replay`. Cost once committed: a ~130-hr per-game-hydrate backfill over 12 seasons (the ingest/parse/resolve path must be **built first** — no script exists). **Storage shape (leaning B):** emit one synthetic `lineup_stints` row per unit-per-game (`opp_lineup` empty, possessions/points from the object, `source='natstat_lineups'`) so the existing aggregate + on/off SQL runs unchanged — `lineup_stints` is local-only and fully regenerated each compute, so synthetic rows are harmless.
      - **De-risk + ingest SHIPPED 2026-06-10 — the gate PASSED, with three live surprises the loader now encodes.** (1) Units carry exact NatStat player *codes*, but the code series doesn't always match our box-ingested era (NatStat re-issues player ids — a game's lineup codes can be an entirely different block from its box codes), so resolution is **two-tier and game-scoped**: exact code match against the game's box roster first, then the abbreviated `lineupplayers` name (`"F. Abee · J. Banks · …"`); ambiguous abbreviations stay NULL. Measured resolution ≥99.2% on every sampled era (2015/2020/2025/2026). (2) The hydrate must be **lineups-only** — `games;playbyplay,lineups` HTTP-500s on 2026 games — and is **v4-pinned** (`NatStatClient::get_v4_only`; api3 doesn't serve hydrates, and the client's sticky v3 fallback would otherwise poison a multi-hour sweep after one flaky 500). (3) The feed **swaps the two team codes on some games** (observed live: every unit of a 2025 FAU/Indiana State game attributed to the wrong side), so each unit resolves against both game rosters and the better-resolving team wins (`team_swapped` in the report). **Storage went durable-capture rather than synthetic-stints-only**: `natstat_lineups` + the `natstat_lineup_games` fetch ledger persist parsed units + full raw JSONB locally (excluded from `sync_to_prod.sh`), so the ~130-hr fetch is spent exactly once and compute re-emits stints FROM the table (the leaning-B synthetic rows would have been wiped by the next compute). `no_lineups` games are real absences (non-D1 opponents have no lineup data). **Backfill kicked off 2026-06-10**, order 2025→2015 then 2026, restart-safe via the ledger; **2019 is included** — the lineups object is server-computed and unaffected by 2019's corrupt PBP tag feed, so 2019 gets lineup data despite its gated PBP. Remaining: the compute-side source swap (re-emit `lineup_stints`/aggregates/on-off from `natstat_lineups`) once the backfill lands enough seasons. **Rate-limit reality (observed 2026-06-10):** NatStat's 500/hr is currently unmetered on v4 — confirmed 1:1 against the account dashboard (only v3 calls deplete; the in-response `ratelimit-remaining` is pinned at 500 on both hosts) — so the sweep runs above the nominal cap via `NATSTAT_MAX_PER_HOUR`, guarded by a `throttle-level` warning in the client and a 429/`OUT_OF_CALLS` clean-abort in the sweep (ledger intact, resumable). Expect the gap to close when v4 leaves beta. *(Addendum 2026-06-10: NatStat DOES push back above the nominal rate — sustained 1500/hr drew `throttle-level=1` (slowed responses, no hard block); the sweep dropped back to 500/hr. The throttle warning works as designed.)*
      - **Compute-side source swap SHIPPED 2026-06-11 — coherence-gated per team-game, NOT the planned blanket swap, because the lineups object turned out era-incomplete.** Probing the first ~2,700 captured games refuted the de-risk's "exact for all seasons" headline: unit points sum to the box score on **2020-era games (~91% avg coverage; 78% of sampled team-games within ±5%)** but only **~38% on 2025/2026** (median 16 units/game vs 53–57 — NatStat appears to compute modern units from its sparse onfloor stream), and **2015 OVER-counts (~109%, overlapping unit windows)**. Two further mapping facts: NatStat's per-unit `possessions` is **not our possession unit** (sums to ~55–66% of the box estimate even on coherent games — each team-game's unit possessions are rescaled to the box-score estimate, putting natstat ortg/drtg on the AdjO/AdjD scale; defensive possessions derive from `points-d / dppp`, rescaled to the opponent's estimate), and the object has **no clock** (stint seconds are possession-share estimates; the box-minute validity clamps are skipped for this source since membership is exact). The swap in `compute_pbp_lineups`: a team-game sources from `natstat_lineups` only when **Σ unit points ≈ box score (±5%) AND slot resolution ≥0.9** (`natstat_covered_team_games`); covered sides skip replay entirely (zero double-count, verified), everything else stays replay/onfloor. **Corrupt-2019 path: the corrupt gate now only blocks REPLAY** — gated natstat team-games still emit, so 2019 gets lineups/on-off for the first time once its backfill lands. Source labels flow through `lineup_stints`/`lineup_aggregates`/`player_on_off` as `'natstat_lineups'` (priority natstat > onfloor > replay, the existing best-source-seen convention). Verified on the captured samples: 2020 → 39 team-games swapped, possession rescale lands exactly on the box estimate (0.00% error), team ortg avg exactly 100/100 poss, on/off invariants + possession parity suites pass, zero mixed-source team-games. **Consequence for the workstream: the cross-season-consistent exact-lineup dream is dead as stated** — the value concentrates in the coherent middle era, while 2025/2026 stay replay/onfloor and 2015 likely stays replay. Tier-3 RAPM-on-lineups inherits the same gate. *(Era update as the reordered backfill lands, 2026-06-10 late: coverage is a GRADIENT, not a cliff — 2024 averages ~65% points coverage (between 2020's ~91% and 2025's ~38%, uniform across Nov/Dec) and passes the gate at only ~7.5% of team-games, so the high-yield exact era is likely ≤2023; boundary still descending with the backfill.)*
      - **Membership-derived model features backtested 2026-06-11 — split verdict: `lineup_quality` REJECTED for game models, prior-season on/off POSITIVE for trajectory** (full numbers in `training/eval_history/tier2_membership_models_20260611_summary.json`). Pre-req bug fix shipped first: `player_on_off` OFF rates were guarded by `nullif(x, 0)`, which passes ~1e-13 float-residual possession sums while integer points stay nonzero — minting ~1e16 ratings — and six seasons (2016–2018, 2021–2023) still carried stale rows predating the on/off rewrite's ≥100-possession ON gate. Fixed with a **≥10-possession floor on OFF-rate denominators** in `compute.rs` + an 11-season recompute (post-fix: max |net_on_off| 259, zero sub-gate rows). **Game models** (`experiment_game_lineups.py`, +3 leak-free expanding diffs — possession HHI, top-lineup share, top-lineup net, via `features.py::compute_cumulative_team_lineups` behind `LINEUP_FEATURES=1`): all 7 metrics degrade on the shared last-20%-of-2026 holdout (margin MAE 8.119 → 8.149) while the diffs ranked 10/11/15 of 52 by importance — the same importance-is-not-value trap as Tier-1; continuity/concentration is absorbed by the AdjEM/roster channels. **Trajectory** (`experiment_trajectory_onoff.py`, +3: `prior_on_net_rtg`, `prior_net_on_off`, `prior_on_poss_share`, −999 sentinel): pooled LOPO MAE 2.135 → 2.127, covered-subset 2.149 → 2.138 (−0.011), **9/11 pairs win and all four class-year buckets improve** — categorically unlike every rejected family (flat-to-negative, ~5/11). On/off carries team-context impact evidence the box score and CamPom genuinely lack. Modest magnitude, so shipping is a deliberate call: it changes the trajectory wire contract (48 → 51 features; retrain, ONNX re-export, Rust `TRAJECTORY_FEATURE_NAMES`, serving-side `player_on_off` lookup with sentinel fill) — its own PR if accepted. `LINEUP_FEATURES` plumbing stays gated off; both scripts kept as re-test harnesses (re-run the game one if natstat exact-membership coverage matures the stint quality).
      - **Trajectory on/off features ACCEPTED + SHIPPED 2026-06-11 — the 48→51 wire-contract change, end-to-end.** The three accepted features moved into the production contract: `train_trajectory_model.py`'s `PAIRED_QUERY` carries the `player_on_off` LEFT JOIN natively (NUMERIC head 25→28, −999 sentinel where the rollup has no row; the experiment harness now *ablates* them from production rather than re-adding). Production retrain on the same corpus reproduced the accept exactly — **pooled LOPO MAE 2.133 → 2.127, covered-subset 2.138, every class-year bucket improves, elite-tail bias unchanged** — and the final fit ranks `prior_on_net_rtg` **3rd** and `prior_net_on_off` **6th** of 51 by importance (on/off coverage 89.5% of paired rows). Rust side: `TRAJECTORY_FEATURE_NAMES`/`TRAJECTORY_HEAD_FEATURES` extended (recruit block stays the trailing slice, lockstep test untouched), `TrajectoryPlayerRow` + both fetch queries grew the `player_on_off` join with the same SQL expressions as training, sentinel fill in `build_trajectory_features` (0.0 is a *meaningful* on/off value, so missingness gets its own encoding — unlike the 0.0-filled box stats). Boot validator accepts the new meta; live serve verified for both a covered player and a no-rollup sentinel player. **Downstream regenerated in order**: trajectory OOF re-persisted (24,418 rows) → roster-impact retrain (pooled LOSO 3.67 → 3.70, within noise; LOSO per-season ONNX re-exported) → 11-season honest backtest re-dumped (**pooled roster_proj MAE 6.04 → 5.96**; delta also includes data drift since the prior dump — lineups backfill + the on/off recompute — so not purely feature-attributable) → CAE recomputed off the new dump (ICC 0.137, split-half +0.216, all guards pass; was 0.157/+0.253) → `compute-projections` rematerialized 2024–2026 (313/285/284 rows). One cohort note: Cal St. Bakersfield 2026 moved into the standing too-thin-roster skip bucket under the new OOF. The PBP model-feature ledger now stands at: Tier-1 tags rejected, lineup-quality rejected, **on/off → trajectory shipped** — next membership play is RAPM.
      - **Tier-1 KEY-RULE verification — DONE 2026-06-09 (the gate that cleared Tier-1 to ship first).** "Skew-free" previously meant only *lineup-source independent*; the KEY RULE additionally demands the **CSV (train) and API (serve) tag streams be equivalent**, which had never been checked. Verified four ways: (1) both loaders produce identical `PbpRow.tags` via the same `parse_tags`; (2) the API feed carries the **identical tag vocabulary** as the CSV (`2ch,3FA,brk,FGA,FOULED,FTA,offto,paint`); (3) **2026 is already serving API-sourced tags on prod** — the onfloor backfill's `replace_game_pbp` swapped them in, so this is observed, not predicted; (4) a **within-2026 controlled comparison** (same season, loader the only variable) shows CSV-sourced games (93) vs API-sourced (6,015) at statistically equivalent derived coverage — paint **72.5% vs 73.9%**, transition 31.0 vs 31.8, off-TO 37.5 vs 39.6, fouls-drawn 62.5 vs 64.6 → **no source skew**. The 2027 nightly cron uses the same `ingest_play_by_play_by_date` path, so it produces the same tags. **Caveat (a feature-encoding rule, not a blocker):** tag *density* varies by **season** (2025 ~53% paint vs 2026 ~74% — a season effect, independent of source), so Tier-1 features **must be rate/share-encoded** (`paint_fga / fga`), never raw counts, else a model trained across seasons and served at current-season density carries a mild bias. **Consequence:** Tier-1 is **decoupled** from the Tier-2 source decision (the tag stream is settled *and* cron-safe) and can proceed now; only the membership half (`on/off`, `plus_minus_pbp`) waits on the source lock. *(Doc-staleness note: the `docs/pbp_utilization_scope.md` inventory line "tags from CSV (all seasons)" is now wrong for 2026 — its tags are API-sourced — correct when next editing.)*
      - **Tier-1 integration plan.** Three surfaces, all on rate-encoded forms per the caveat above:
        - [x] **DB — SHIPPED 2026-06-09 (migration `036`).** Season-level PBP RATE columns on `player_season_stats` (`paint_rate`, `paint_fg_pct`, `perimeter_fg_pct`, and `transition`/`second_chance`/`points_off_turnovers`/`fouls_drawn`_per40) rolled up in `compute_pbp_aggregates` from the per-game tag columns (over PBP-covered games only, so each rate's numerator/denominator share a game set; grouped by (player_id, team_id) to match the season-stat grain). Matching `*_pct` percentiles in `compute_player_percentiles`, ranked over **non-NULL values only** — `(rank()-1)/(count(non-NULL)-1)`, not a plain PERCENT_RANK, so the no-PBP tail doesn't compress the scale (negligible with a full season, but load-bearing in-season when the partition is mostly sparse). Within-season percentile IS the cross-season density normalization. Both tables ship to prod. Verified on 2026: rates sane (avg paint_rate .52, paint FG% .54), percentiles span 0–1, corruption-gate clears them.
        - [x] **Web UI — SHIPPED 2026-06-09.** `GET /api/players/:id/pbp` enriched with the rate values + percentiles (primary-team row, matching the percentile pass's DISTINCT-ON choice); the PlayerDetail "Play-by-Play Profile" panel now shows per-40 rates colored by percentile + rim/jumper FG% with percentile ranks + paint-rate percentile — closing the "raw counts, not comparable, redundant with shot-diet" gap. Verified: AJ Dybantsa transition 97th, fouls-drawn 99th, paint FG% 77th.
        - [x] **Models — DONE 2026-06-10, all three verdicts REJECTED (backtested; nothing shipped to serving).** Rolled rate-encoded tag aggregates into each model family behind its own backtest; every one came back negative, so Tier-1 closes as a **serving-only** feature set. The consistent story: CamPom/GBPM already absorb whatever *value* signal the style rates carry — a style rate that doesn't predict value is still a good descriptive surface, which is what shipped in the DB/UI pass. (a) **Game models (margin/win/total)**: built the leak-free expanding **team-level** PBP-rate diffs (paint rate, paint/perimeter FG%, transition/2nd-chance/off-TO shares, fouls drawn per 100) in `features.py::compute_cumulative_team_pbp`, gated behind `PBP_FEATURES` (default off). Shared chronological 2026 holdout (n=925): margin MAE **8.082 → 8.157 (+0.075 WORSE)**, win-from-margin acc 0.741 → 0.732, AUC ~flat; best_iter 625 → 246 (the diffs add noise that stops boosting earlier). `diff_pbp_perimeter_fg_pct` ranked 10/56 by importance while degrading accuracy — importance ≠ value. (b) **Trajectory**: prior-season rates as features, both raw and percentile encodings, −1 missing sentinel; LOPO pooled MAE 2.1367 (baseline) vs 2.1395/2.1403 — both worse, per-pair wins 5/11, 4/11. Freshman skipped by design (recruits have no college PBP history). (c) **Archetypes**: +5 style axes on the combined 12-season cohort → **9 signature violations (baseline 0)** and covered-era stability 0.484 → 0.469 while pre-2020 artificially inflates 0.520 → 0.555 — the mean-imputation era artifact from the 2020+ coverage wall, unfixable at the source. **Kept**: the gated `features.py` plumbing + the three `training/experiment_*_pbp.py` harnesses as the re-test path if the data changes (e.g. richer per-lineup tags from the Tier-2 `lineups`-object ingest). Membership-derived features (on/off → models) were excluded throughout — they wait on the Tier-2 source lock, and remain the PBP model play with real a-priori upside (with RAPM behind them).
  - [ ] **Possession & lineup data — feature incorporation (brainstorm, 2026-06-07)** — how the P3 possession/rate data (shipped) and the discovered API-native lineup/perf fields (above) plug into existing site surfaces. Ordered by value/effort:
    - [x] **Player on/off impact** *(team rtg with vs without a player, per 100 poss)* — the headline player-value narrative KenPom/EvanMiya lead with. **SHIPPED 2026-06-07** (item **A**): prod-resident `player_on_off` rollup (migration `034`) derived in `compute_pbp_lineups` off the validity-clamped game-lineups, `GET /api/players/:id/on-off`, and the "On / Off Splits" panel on PlayerDetail. Raw `lineup_stints` stays local. Replay-sourced until the onfloor backfill lands per season.
    - [x] **Adjusted +/- (RAPM) — the principled fix for raw on/off** *(scoped 2026-06-07 off the on/off spot-check; COMPLETE 2026-06-12 — shipped narrowed as "Adj on/off (RAPM)", see the verdict below)* — raw on/off is a **contextual team-result** stat, not an individual-skill one: it conflates a player with his teammates, his opponents, and *when* he plays. The shipped on/off makes this vivid — **Zuby Ejiofor (St. John's 2026) posts CamPom +20.8 but on/off −8.4** because the team was +22 when he sat: a deep, dominant team's bench pads its net rating in blowout garbage time, dragging every starter's off-court number up (St. John's whole starting core reads negative while the bench reads positive). The literature's fix is **regularized adjusted +/- (RAPM)**: ridge-regress per-stint point margin (per 100 poss) on the on-floor indicator vector for *all 10 players*, with an L2 prior shrinking unknowns toward 0 — solving for each player's partial effect *holding teammates and opponents constant*. We now have every input: per-stint 5-on-5 membership + possessions (`lineup_stints`, P3) across 2015–2026. **This is the unblocked version of the "Native cstat player impact metric" item below — same goal (a descriptive cstat-native value grade), now tractable at stint resolution.** **→ DESIGN DOC + SPIKE COMPLETE 2026-06-12: REJECTED as a standalone value metric** (`docs/rapm_methodology.md` §8; harness `training/experiment_rapm_spike.py`, summaries in eval_history). The doc's spec: two-offense-row weighted ridge (per-100, possession-weighted, O/D blocks + intercept + HCA), zero prior + CamPom-prior variants, game-blocked λ CV, per-season fits; substrate findings: replay carries both-side 5-man pairing in *every* season (~140k–230k paired stints/season — NOT blocked on the backfill), natstat units are unpaired (not RAPM rows), and the Tier-2 swap would evaporate covered-era paired stints (the `replay_shadow` mitigation is specified but now suspended with the rest). **Spike verdict:** both kill-gates failed — held-out stint MSE loses to a fair team-columns ridge (5152.5 best vs 5147.0), and YoY returner stability ρ +0.16..+0.27 is nowhere near CamPom's +0.72..+0.77 — while the allocation mechanism itself *works*: 2–5× the stability of raw on/off swing (+0.24 vs +0.11; +0.16 vs +0.05) and the Zuby test passes emphatically (Ejiofor on/off −10.6 → net RAPM +4.8; all 5 St. John's core players flip positive). **→ Narrowed scope APPROVED + SHIPPED same day as "Adj on/off (RAPM)"** (doc §8.1): the zero-prior fit (λ=1000, 52,421 `player_rapm` rows × 11 seasons via `training/rapm.py` + migration 038) serves as the context-adjusted companion line in the PlayerDetail on/off panel AND replaces the raw on/off column on all three grids — TeamDetail roster, Players, Transfer portal — with raw on/off moving to the tooltips (≥250 paired-poss display floor everywhere; API routes carry `rapm_*`); the `replay_shadow` compute change protects the paired-stint corpus from the Tier-2 source swap (2020-verified: served surfaces checksum-identical, parity suite green). **Trajectory-slot test (doc §8.2): REJECTED both ways** — swapping the shipped on/off block for RAPM is decisively worse (pooled LOPO 2.1282 → 2.1382, 1/11 pairs; raw on/off's team-context is *signal* for next-season CamPom), adding it is noise (+0.0009); the 51-feature contract stands. Re-test triggers: multi-season pooled design or materially better stint data (the lineups backfill does not help — unpaired). The sketch bullets below are superseded by the doc. Sketch / open questions:
      - **Design matrix**: one row per stint (or per possession), columns = all players (+1 home on-floor, −1 away on-floor), target = margin per 100. Cross-season → either per-season fits or a multi-season ridge with season fixed effects. Millions of stint rows × ~5k players/season → sparse solve (scikit `Ridge`/`lsqr` or a custom conjugate-gradient).
      - **Regularization**: the λ prior IS the method — too low overfits low-minute players, too high crushes everyone to 0. Tune by held-out stint prediction (predict next-game margins). Optionally a **Bayesian prior centered on CamPom/box-RAPM** (BBR-style) so priors are informative, not just 0 — pairs naturally with the box stats we already have.
      - **Offense/defense split**: separate ORAPM/DRAPM by regressing offensive vs defensive possessions (the on/off table already carries `on/off_possessions_for/against`), recovering the defensive half raw +/- can't isolate.
      - **Data-quality dependency**: RAPM is sensitive to lineup accuracy, so it wants the **onfloor backfill** (P-onfloor) more than the raw on/off did — replay drift becomes regression noise. Prioritize onfloor-exact seasons for the first fit; quantify replay-vs-onfloor RAPM divergence on 2026 once both exist.
      - **Acceptance**: beats raw on/off and matches/beats CamPom on (a) year-over-year stability for returning players and (b) external benchmarks (KenPom POY / AP All-American); and Zuby-type stars stop reading negative once teammates/opponents are controlled. Multi-PR; needs its own design doc (`docs/rapm_methodology.md`). **Soft-blocked on**: onfloor backfill for clean lineups (nice-to-have, not strict) + a sparse-solver spike. See also [[the Native cstat player impact metric item below]] — fold the two together when this kicks off.

Spike record: method validated, model trials not pursued (verdict `docs/rapm_methodology.md` §10.7; harness `experiment_rapm_pooled.py`). Prequentially (fit through t → predict t+1 stints), pooled beats single-season RAPM AND carried-over team strength on all three targets — but clipped CamPom-carryover still edges it (5193.4 vs 5194.5), so the value-metric verdict stands. Split-half reliability +30–50% (net 0.295→0.382; **D-RAPM confirmed the noisy side**, 0.189→0.282); star-compression improves modestly (≥+15 gap −0.099→−0.073); flat pooling (decay 1.0) won monotonically, sharpening the display tension (prequential-optimal = most current-form blur; decay 0.7/w3/λ2k is the ship compromise). Side-discovery: the cam_o/cam_d garbage tails that zeroed the spike's CamPom baseline — root-caused to the signed-share SOS allocation and **fixed at the source** (the companion ship above). *(Original design below; both open decisions from the spike — display swap, model trials — are resolved above.)* (`docs/rapm_methodology.md` §10). Single-season RAPM's failure was identification (~350–500 paired poss/player → ridge crushes the top of the scale: CamPom ≥8 players sit 10–18 percentile points below their CamPom rank, compressing within-team star-vs-6th-man gaps). Pooling is the standard lever (NBA practice = 3-year RAPM). Design: career chains via the dual key (natstat_id OR torvik_pid, ~96% coverage; singletons degrade honestly), shape (c) decayed-window refit first (per-season output, single solve) with shape (b) season-coupled fused ridge as the principled follow-up; 2019 bridged by the coupling. **The honesty trap is pre-registered**: pooled YoY Spearman is mechanically inflated (adjacent estimates share data), so the acceptance suite replaces it with a prequential test (pooled estimate through season t predicts season t+1 held-out stint margins vs single-season RAPM / team ridge / CamPom-as-predictor), split-half reliability, the star-separation-gap diagnostic, and the Zuby test. **Surfaces if accepted** (each separately gated): display upgrade of the served Adj on/off values (the likely win — same `player_rapm` table); trajectory feature re-trial via the existing harness (LOW prior — pooling doesn't restore the context that made raw on/off win); roster-impact aggregate experiment (the most interesting model slot — that pipeline is information-limited and pooled RAPM is partially box-independent); game models explicitly NOT planned (team-level absorbed). PR plan: spike `experiment_rapm_pooled.py` (go/no-go) → conditional shape (b) → conditional production/display swap → conditional model trials. Not blocked on anything external.

Now that the splits are trustworthy and displayed, the candidate next steps, each separately gated: **(a) ~~Re-run `validation/exp_campom_od_architecture.py` on the FIXED splits~~ DONE 2026-06-12** — the contamination suspicion confirmed: on fixed targets the primitives margin shrinks from "8–32%" to 1.7–6.4%, and that residual edge exists **only with oracle N+1 SOS**. With the SOS you'd actually have at serve time (prior-season), PRIMITIVES *loses* to DIRECT on net (2.171 vs 2.140 MAE) and cam_o (1.796 vs 1.777) and ties on cam_d (0.969 vs 0.967). **Architecture verdict: the formula-as-inductive-bias edge was an SOS-oracle artifact — serve-time O/D projection should be DIRECT or NET+SPLIT, not primitive composition** (NET+SPLIT costs +0.033 cam_d MAE vs DIRECT but reconciles exactly: O+D ≡ net). Same run also answered the RAPM question — see (d). **(b) Trajectory CPO/CPD projection targets** — project the next-season O/D split and display it as a "projected O/D" sub-line wherever the projection chip appears (PlayerDetail, Future tab, portal Projection column's tooltip). Per the (a) verdict, the right shape is **NET+SPLIT anchored to the served trajectory net**: one new small model predicts next-season cam_o, cam_d derives as `trajectory_mean − cam_o_proj`, so the displayed split can never diverge from the served net projection (the q10–q90 band stays net-only). Candidate extra features for the O-model: the O/D-decomposed on/off swings (`on_ortg−off_ortg`, `on_drtg−off_drtg`) — the (d) run showed on/off carrying real signal (−0.016 net MAE over base) where RAPM added ~nothing on top. This is also the load-bearing input the refuted-as-coach-metric CAE-O/D item said was missing (projected AdjO/AdjD needs O/D-decomposed roster projections — option (b) of that item's design). **(c) Projected team AdjO/AdjD decomposition — VALIDATED + SHIPPED 2026-06-12** (`validation/exp_team_adjod_projection.py`, LOSO over the served roster-impact feature frame, 4,255 team-seasons). Unlike the player-cam case, the team level is structurally *friendly*: AdjEM = AdjO − AdjD holds to |resid| 0.025 (exact reconciliation), and the halves are **negatively** correlated (r = −0.60 — good teams are high-O AND low-D), so EM has *more* spread than either half (SD 15.1 vs 8.9/8.1) and is the easiest target. The same features project both halves at ~51% skill (AdjO MAE 3.42 / AdjD 3.25 vs naive 7.06/6.61; net AdjEM 3.74 @ 69.6%). **Decomposing does NOT hurt the served net**: DIRECT-both (net = AdjO_pred − AdjD_pred) is only +0.008 MAE worse than the served net, because the O/D errors are *positively* correlated (+0.36) and cancel in the difference — the opposite of the player-cam var-compounding trap. Recommended shape: **NET+SPLIT anchored to the served AdjEM** (predict AdjO with one model, derive AdjD = AdjO_pred − AdjEM_served) → served net untouched, exact reconciliation, AdjD MAE 3.28 (≈ direct 3.25). Display-only descriptive bands beneath the "Proj AdjEM" headline; never a coach grade (the CAE-O/D refutation stands — that was attribution, not projectability). **SHIPPED 2026-06-12**: `train_roster_adjo_model.py` trains the AdjO half on the served roster-impact feature frame (LOSO MAE 3.37, 52% skill, matches the experiment) → `roster_adjo_model.onnx` (+ meta, reuses the ROSTER_IMPACT feature contract); `Predictor::predict_roster_adjo` loads it (boot-validated); `projections.rs` runs both models per scenario, shrinks AdjO toward prior-season `adj_offense` at the same weight, blends to the midpoint, and derives `projected_adj_d = projected_adj_o − midpoint_adj_em` (served net untouched, reconciles exactly — live-verified 0/251 reconciliation failures). Surfaced as **two dedicated sortable columns — "Proj AdjO" / "Proj AdjD"** — immediately after Proj AdjEM, each a value plus a colored "#rank" subscript (the Rankings-page `RankedCell`/`pctileTextColor` convention; AdjO ranks high-first, AdjD low-first). **(c-freshman) Freshman CPO/CPD projection — NOT worth it** (`validation/exp_freshman_od_projection.py`, LOCO, 3,263 freshmen): net freshman CamPom is already only 6.4% skill over the tier-mean baseline (recruit→production is intrinsically noisy), and the halves are weaker still — CPO 3.2%, CPD 4.7% (CPD SD just 1.58; a 247 number barely predicts freshman defense). Too thin to display honestly; leave freshman projection net-only. **(d) RAPM-O/RAPM-D as model features — stays CLOSED, now with the O/D-target data point** (2026-06-12, same run as (a)): in a box-feature-only harness pooled RAPM-O/D showed a small consistent lift (−0.002…−0.006 MAE, cam_d 5/5 folds; `f_rapm_o` the useful half at gain-rank 7–11, `f_rapm_d` rank 16–25 — the noisy side again), but the decisive incremental test — base+raw-on/off vs base+raw-on/off+RAPM, i.e. beyond what the production trajectory contract already carries — left only **−0.003 MAE on every target (4/5 folds)**, and the production 51-feature set is far stronger than this harness's base, so even that would shrink. Consistent with §10.7's swap/add verdict; re-test triggers unchanged. Display-side, all four columns shipped 2026-06-12 (Players/portal CPO+CPD; roster Adv view all four + RAPM net); 2026-06-12 also added PlayerCompare CPO/CPD rows, the Predict roster-chip split tooltip, PlayerProgression CPO/CPD rows (replacing its GBPM/OGBPM/DGBPM rows — GBPM bars/rows also removed from PlayerDetail and PlayerCompare; CamPom family is the canonical impact display), CPO/CPD on the Draft board (envelope-gated, on the shared diverging gradient); and the Future page's cohort O/D decoration: prior-season `*_cam_o_sum`/`*_cam_d_sum` per cohort (envelope-gated, COALESCE-0 convention) in the flow-column tooltips plus two dedicated sortable **"Roster O" / "Roster D"** columns (Σ over returners + transfers, value + colored #rank subscript). Future-page polish in the same pass: the stale "Δ vs last" column removed, light gray group-divider lines (body rows) between the projection / actual / context / roster-shape / roster-flow column blocks, and `rowHeight` 48 for the two-line rank cells — all display-only, the served net projection untouched.

This **supersedes** the originally-scoped per-player `GET /api/players/:id/lineups` — the cross-team endpoint with a `player` filter covers the per-player drill-down too.
      - **Rating methodology** (a spot-check found the raw-net top-10 dominated by below-average mid-majors — SIU Edwardsville AdjEM −7.4, Utah Valley +2.7 — whose best lineup just beat a weak schedule). Fix: each lineup's per-100 rates are **opponent-adjusted** to the KenPom AdjO / AdjD / AdjEM scale by shifting them with the team's schedule adjustment on each side (`team adj_offense − team raw offense`, and likewise defense; raw offense/defense computed inline from `team_game_stats` box possessions). `adj_net = adj_ortg − adj_drtg`; the page ranks by it. **Sample noise is handled by a size-scaled minutes floor, NOT shrinkage** — once the schedule adjustment is in, the high-`adj_net` combos *are* the strong teams, so a light floor (5-man 100 / trio 200 / duo 300; duos/trios pool minutes across every 5-man they're in) keeps thin outliers out while leaving blue-bloods in. (An earlier empirical-Bayes shrinkage pass was dropped once the opponent adjustment made it redundant — and a *high* floor alone is wrong: only 11 lineups clear 200 min in 2025 and tight-rotation mid-majors over-accumulate exact-5-man minutes.) Raw `net_rtg`/`ortg`/`drtg` still returned for the AdjEM tooltip. 2025 5-man top: Clemson / Marquette / Houston / St John's / Creighton / Florida / Duke; duos/trios: Auburn / Kentucky / Florida / Alabama.

Validation: two consecutive freshman retrains produce bit-identical `freshman_model_meta.json` and identical predictions; the underlying ONNX bytes still differ at byte ~228568 because skl2onnx embeds non-deterministic export metadata (producer version / timestamp in the protobuf) — model weights themselves are deterministic. Result: future retrains produce clean meta-diffs (changes mean real changes, not stochastic noise), and historical models can be reproduced from source. The shipped models in this commit are the canonical seeded baseline; prior unpinned weights are not recoverable but the differences were within fold-noise (<0.05 MAE).

The Le Moyne 2026 ghost row was deleted along with the others. **Follow-up next offseason (2027 prep)**: check whether NatStat retroactively fills 2026 once Le Moyne completes its 4-year transition; if so, the next `season --year 2026` ingest will recreate the row cleanly. - [x] **Native cstat player impact metric** (alternative to Torvik GBPM passthrough). **CLOSED 2026-06-12 via the RAPM verdict** (this item folded into the Adjusted +/- (RAPM) item above, which was its post-PBP form): the stint-resolution attribution was built and backtested — single-season RAPM loses to a team-columns ridge on held-out stints and reads YoY ρ +0.16..+0.27 vs CamPom's +0.72..+0.77, so a cstat-native value grade does NOT beat the CamPom passthrough at our data resolution. The acceptance bar this item set (match/beat CamPom on stability + external benchmarks) is exactly what failed. What survived is display-scoped: "Adj on/off (RAPM)" in the on/off panel. Re-open only behind the doc's re-test triggers (multi-season pooled design / materially better stint data) — see `docs/rapm_methodology.md` §8. Frame as a *descriptive* grade — "what's this player's value, derived purely from cstat's own machinery" — not a predictor (the §4f experiment already showed CamPom-style adjustments don't beat raw GBPM as ML features; this is a different goal). Approach: non-linear regression of team-game outcomes on roster-composition features (player IDs × minutes) to attribute per-player coefficients. Acceptance: matches or beats CamPom on (a) year-over-year rank stability for returning players and (b) external benchmarks (KenPom POY, AP All-American). **Caveat — re-attempting failed work**: cstat's prior native BPM (`compute.rs` pre-PR #25) tried this with linear box-score formulas and got r=0.075 with Torvik OBPM. Don't repeat that approach; the limit at our data resolution *as of today* is team-game level (no play-by-play) and box-score-derived linear coefficients are exactly what blew up. A LightGBM-on-team-game-outcomes attribution is a different methodology and worth trying — but it's a multi-PR project and would need its own design doc. **PBP changes the picture**: once `play_by_play` lands (Refactor Backlog) and lineup stints exist, the attribution problem becomes per-stint (5-on-5 with known on-floor players over a known time window), which is what most impact metrics in the literature actually use. The native impact metric is far more tractable post-PBP — consider this item soft-blocked on the PBP ingest, then revisit with stint-level RAPM or a Bayesian adjusted +/- as the starting methodology. **As of 2026-06-07 the PBP blocker is cleared** (stints + possessions + on/off shipped), so this is now the same project as the **"Adjusted +/- (RAPM)"** item in the PBP feature-incorporation list above — start there; fold this item into it.

## Phase 7: Off-Season Fun & Engagement
> Give people a reason to visit in July. A cluster of lightweight games, quizzes, and toys that lean into the one thing that makes cstat unique — the 12 D&D-class archetype system — and otherwise reuse surfaces we've already built (percentile radars, shot diets, CamPom, the prediction engine, `similar`, OG cards). None of this is served-critical; all of it is read-only against existing tables, so it carries no ingest/compute risk. Sequence descriptive-first: ship the daily game (habit), then the quiz (viral funnel), then the toys.

Naming note: the daily game shipped as **Mystery Baller** (chosen 2026-07-01) and was **renamed to Portle** shortly after. The quiz and matchup toys can share the eventual brand. Route is `/portle` (legacy `/mystery-baller` 301s to it, preserving practice-share query params); code lives in `pages/Portle.tsx`, `lib/portle.ts`, `lib/portle.test.ts`, `components/portle/GuessGrid.tsx`. The internal `mb:` localStorage keys were deliberately kept so the rename doesn't reset players' streaks. The historical section below is left under the old name as a shipped record; substitute Portle throughout.

### 7a: Daily Player Guessing Game ("Mystery Baller", now "Portle") — v1 SHIPPED 2026-07-01
Page title is just **Mystery Baller** (CamPom is already in the navbar); the **share header is branded `CamPom · Mystery Baller`** via `GAME_TITLE` in `lib/mysteryBaller.ts`, using the same ` · ` separator as the rest of the header. A daily player-guessing game. **Model: grid + opt-in hint reveals** (grid+reveal hybrid decided 2026-07-01; blur replaced by opt-in dropdowns after playtest — CSS blur didn't hide the radar/shot shapes). The core is a Poeltl-style **attribute grid** — each guess drops a row of colored cells (Team · Conference · Class year · Height · Archetype · PPG · Usage% · CamPom) with match/direction feedback vs the answer; guessed names + the revealed answer link to their player pages (new tab, so the game isn't lost); alongside it two **collapsible hint panels** (archetype radar + shot-diet court) the player can optionally expand, each opened one costing the "clean solve". **Solve in 10 tries**, or **Give up** to reveal. **Mostly client-side** — every grid attribute is on the `GET /api/players` list row; a small backend add put `height_inches` on that row (Rust `PlayerRow` + list SELECT) so the Height column works without a per-guess fetch; the hint panels do one detail fetch of the answer (`GET /api/players/{id}` → `torvik_stats`). Files: `pages/MysteryBaller.tsx` (parent + keyed `MysteryGame` child + `HintPanel`), `components/mysteryBaller/GuessGrid.tsx`, `components/PlayerPicker.tsx` (extracted), `lib/mysteryBaller.ts` (pure logic), `lib/localStore.ts`, `lib/mysteryBaller.test.ts` (Vitest, 21 tests). `tsc`/`eslint`/`build` + `cargo check` clean.

- [x] **Pool load** — `fetchPlayers({ season, limit: 5000 })` on the season, mirroring `Players.tsx` (the app has no React-Query provider; pages fetch directly). A `poolSeason` guard prevents a mid-flight season change from seeding off the wrong season. Endpoint already hard-gates `GP>=5 & MPG>=10`. Optional later optimization: a lean `mystery-pool` projection returning only the game columns.
- [x] **Daily answer selection — server-authoritative pin (#181)** — the daily answer is chosen and **frozen server-side**, so every client fetches the identical puzzle and it never moves once set. `GET /api/portle/daily?mode=&season=&date=` → `queries::pick_or_pin_daily_puzzle`: the eligible player whose `md5("mode:season:date:" + natstat_id)` sorts first is INSERTed into `portle_daily_puzzle (mode, season, puzzle_date, natstat_id)` on first request (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, concurrent-safe) and read back by every later request via the PK. Returns just the stable `natstat_id`; the client resolves it in the pool it already loaded and plays as before. `date` is the player's **local** calendar date (Wordle convention) — same local date → same pin across timezones. The eligible set mirrors `filterPool` EXACTLY on the `/api/players` base (season, GP>=5, MPG>=10, answerable = CamPom + archetype present) so the pin is always a pool member (parity guarded by `crates/cstat-core/tests/portle_daily.rs`). `portle_daily_puzzle` is runtime-written on prod, so it is EXCLUDED from `sync_to_prod.sh` (a sync must never wipe a pin prod already served).
  - **Why server-authoritative** (the history): the pick was originally a pure client-side rendezvous hash `min(hash32("mode:season:date:" + player_id))`. Two bugs surfaced: (1) it keyed on `player_id`, a `gen_random_uuid` surrogate re-minted on any season rebuild + re-COPY'd to prod by `sync_to_prod.sh`, so a data update reset the whole daily sequence — fixed by moving the key to the stable `natstat_id`; (2) during a deploy skew (frontend seeding on `natstat_id` before the API served it) every key was `undefined`, all hashes collapsed, and the pick pinned to `eligible[0]` — the CamPom-sorted pool head — i.e. "always the best player" every day. The client still keeps `poolHasSeedKeys()` as a fail-closed guard (renders "data refreshing" if the pool lacks `natstat_id` rather than resolving a pin off an inconsistent pool), and PRACTICE mode still uses the client-side `pickByHash` rendezvous (per-user roll, cross-client consistency irrelevant). But the daily is no longer computed client-side at all — the server pin is the single source of truth and removes seed skew, UUID churn, and eligible-pool drift outright. (`natstat_id` was added to the Rust `PlayerRow` + list SELECT and the TS `PlayerRow` so both the pin and the client resolve on it.)
- [x] **Guess feedback engine** — pure `compareGuess(guess, answer)`: **archetype** primary-match hit / any primary·secondary overlap close / else miss; **conference / team** exact hit else miss; **class year** exact hit else ordinal arrow (Fr<So<Jr<Sr/Gr); **Height** ▲/▼ (±1″ hit, ±3″ close), shown ft′in″; **PPG / Usage% / CamPom** directional ▲/▼ with a "close" band (PPG ±2, usage ±3pp, CamPom ±2) → amber, tight → green. `usage_rate` is a fraction in the data, rendered as a whole-number percent (0.297 → "30%"). Null attributes render neutral, never green. Grid tints the archetype cell with `CLASS_COLORS`. (Position was designed in but **dropped — `position` is null for every row in the `/api/players` data**; revisit if the ingest ever populates it. Height sampled realistic across the pool, 70–85″.) The guess-autocomplete hides the "· N PPG" trailing stat via `PlayerPicker`'s `hideStats` so it doesn't leak the PPG hint category.
- [x] **Hint reveals + scoring** — two `HintPanel` dropdowns (archetype radar via `resolveAxes` + Recharts; shot-diet court via `ShotDietCourt`/`ShotDistributionBar`). Collapsed while playing behind a "Reveal … — costs your clean solve" button; opening one while in play is tracked in `openHints` and persisted. After the game ends both auto-expand for free. **Daily scoring = guesses to solve (X/8, drives streak + win%) with a second axis: hints used (0–2).** A 0-hint win is a "clean solve" (own stat + share annotation). Post-game viewing never counts.
- [x] **Guess input** — extracted the debounced `PlayerPicker` from `PlayerCompare.tsx` into reusable `components/PlayerPicker.tsx` (200ms debounce, min 2 chars, `/api/players?search=&limit=12`); PlayerCompare refactored onto it (backward-compatible `onAdd`, new `onPick`/`placeholder`). A **Give up** button ends the round and reveals the answer (records a daily loss). Guessed player names in the grid link to their detail pages (new tab).
- [x] **Practice mode** (requested 2026-07-01) — a **Daily/Practice segmented toggle** (matching the Pool selector style, per feedback) + "New game"/"Play again" that re-roll a **random** eligible player via a fresh seed generated in the event handler (`makeSeed()` → `practiceAnswerByNonce`; seeded rather than `Math.random()`-in-render so each session/roll differs — an early bug where the nonce walked a fixed 1,2,3… sequence gave everyone the same order), unlimited replays, does **not** read/write streak stats. Same grid + reveal + share.
- [x] **Shareable result** — Wordle-style emoji grid (🟩/🟨/⬛ per guess), copy-to-clipboard via `navigator.clipboard`, no answer leak. Header names the scope so a practice run can't pass as the daily: `Mystery Baller · 2026-07-01 · Power 5 · 4/8` (daily, with date) vs `· Practice ·`; hinted solves annotate `(N hints)`. Daily shares the bare page URL; **practice shares `?mode=&season=&seed=`** so a friend replays the exact same answer — the seed reproduces the pick client-side without naming the player (a friend opening the link starts in practice on that seed). The date is also shown on the page for daily. (OG-card social preview for a shared result deferred — see stretch.)
- [x] **Persistence** (`localStorage` via new typed `lib/localStore.ts`): `mb:state:{puzzleKey}` (resume the daily game on reload — guesses + status + opened hints), `mb:stats` (played / wins / streak / maxStreak / **cleanWins** / guess-distribution), `mb:config` (last-used mode). Practice writes none of these. Daily restore + practice reset both fall out of `useState` initializers on the keyed `MysteryGame` remount — no state-syncing effects (repo bans `set-state-in-effect`).
- [x] **Difficulty / scope configs** (segmented mode selector; daily seed shared *per config*):
  - [x] **Power-5 only** (default) — `conference ∈ {ACC, BIG10, BIG12, SEC, BIGEAST}` **and `minutes_per_game >= 20`** (drops deep-bench power-conf players). Exact NatStat codes verified against the live `/api/players` data (Pac-12 no longer fields a league, so no PAC12 code; `AMER` is the American, excluded). Filtered client-side.
  - [x] **Starters only** — `minutes_per_game >= 24`.
  - [x] **All D-I** — the full gated pool (hard mode).
  - [x] **Season scope** — current season by default; any season works automatically via the site-wide `?season=` plumbing (`useSeason()`) + the `poolSeason` guard.
  - [ ] **Archetype-locked** (v1.1) — variant where the answer's class is given and you guess within it.
  - [ ] **Multi-year mode** (proposed 2026-07-01) — answer drawn from *any* season, not just the site `?season=`. Needs: multi-season pool fetch (or a lean cross-season endpoint), a **Season column** in the grid (the same human recurs across seasons as distinct rows), and cross-season guess search (current picker is season-scoped). Moderate lift — scoped as a follow-up, not in v1.
- [x] **Routing / nav** — `/mystery-baller` route in `App.tsx` + a "Play" nav link (desktop + mobile) in `Layout.tsx`.
- [x] **Tests** (Vitest — added `vitest` devDep + `npm test` script; first frontend test infra in the repo). `lib/mysteryBaller.test.ts`: `hash32` determinism, `localDateKey`, `filterPool`/`isAnswerable` gating, `dailyAnswer` order-stability + date variance + empty-pool null, `practiceAnswerByNonce` determinism, `compareGuess` per-attribute rules (categorical / class arrow / height arrow + ft-in / numeric band / archetype hit·close·miss / null handling), `buildShare` (scope label + hint annotation), `recordResult` (streak + cleanWins). 21 tests green.
- [x] **Server-pinned puzzle table** — shipped as the daily-answer source of truth (see the "server-authoritative pin" item above): `portle_daily_puzzle` + `GET /api/portle/daily`. This was the Tier-1 immutability fix; it now also underpins any future server-side game state (global solve stats, no-repeat scheduling, archived past puzzles).
- [ ] Stretch / hardening: `POST /guess` server-side validation for true cheat-resistance (the answer id is still sent to the client, so v1 stays devtools-cheatable like any Wordle clone — accepted; the pin table is the prerequisite that's now in place); a no-repeat-within-N-days window (trivial now that pins are persisted — exclude the last N days' pinned `natstat_id`s for that mode/season); OG-card preview for shared results; a guess-distribution histogram in the stats panel.

### 7b: "Which Class Are You?" Personality Quiz — v1 SHIPPED 2026-07-01
A 7-question personality quiz that maps the visitor to one of the 12 archetypes and shows the class blurb + real-player comps. Pure funnel into `/archetypes`, inherently shareable. **Client-only, no new endpoint.** Files: `pages/WhichClass.tsx`, `lib/whichClass.ts` (pure), `lib/whichClass.test.ts` (8 Vitest tests); route `/which-class` + "Quiz" nav link.

- [x] **Question set** — 7 playstyle/personality questions (clutch shot, hype moment, role, defensive identity, off-court vibe, shot diet, statline), 4 options each, each option carrying weights toward 1–2 classes. Tuned so all 12 classes are reachable (guarded by a test). Data lives in `QUIZ` in `lib/whichClass.ts`.
- [x] **Scoring** — `scoreQuiz` sums option weights per class, returns the ranking high→low, ties broken by `CLASS_ORDER` for determinism; `quizResult` takes the top two as primary + secondary. (Went with weighted-sum rather than softmax — same ranking, simpler + directly testable.)
- [x] **Result card** — class-tinted card (`classColor`/`classTagline`), primary + secondary badges, the class tagline, and up to 3 real players who share the result, each `SeasonLink`ed to the player. **Player match prefers dual-class** (players holding both your classes, either slot; label "share both your classes") and **falls back to primary-only** when no dual exists — sourced from `fetchPlayers({archetype: primary, includeSecondaryArchetype: true, limit: 5000})` (full pool so low-CamPom duals aren't truncated) filtered client-side. A **"Show all N →"** deep-links to the Players page with the same filter (`?archetypes={primary},{secondary}&match=all` for duals — AND-mode reproduces the exact set; `?archetypes={primary}` for the fallback). Plus Share / Retake / "Explore all 12 classes →".
- [x] **Shareable** — copy-to-clipboard one-liner branded `CamPom · Which Class Are You? — I'm a {primary} / {secondary}.` + link. (Rich OG-card preview deferred — same follow-up as the Mystery Baller OG card.)
- [x] **UX** — one-page quiz with a progress bar (answered/total); result appears once every question is answered; `Retake` resets.

### 7c: Time Machine — Cross-Season / Cross-Team Fantasy Matchups
Let users pit any two team-seasons against each other in the predictor — 2015 Kentucky vs 2026 Duke. Technically enabled today by season-scoped team UUIDs + the existing `/api/predict` stack; this is a UI affordance plus a small prediction-path consideration.

- [ ] **Cross-season matchup UI** — extend the Predict team pickers so `home` and `away` can each carry their own season (two season selectors, not one site-wide season). Reuse the existing team autocomplete.
- [ ] **Prediction path** — confirm `/api/predict` can accept a per-team season (features are built per team-season already). If the current signature assumes a single `season`, add optional `home_season` / `away_season` params; fall back to `season` when absent (backward compatible).
- [ ] **Honesty labeling** — a cross-era matchup is inherently a fantasy/what-if (no shared schedule, era pace/rule differences). Label it clearly as "Fantasy matchup" and suppress the point-in-time / prior-meetings sections (which assume same-season, real-schedule context). Surface the `prediction_basis` chip as usual.
- [ ] **Deep-linkable** — `/predict?home=...&home_season=2015&away=...&away_season=2026` so results are shareable, matching the existing ticker deep-link pattern.
- [ ] **Discovery entry point** — a "Time Machine" tile/CTA (e.g., "Pick two teams from any two seasons") plus a few curated marquee matchups to seed the idea.

### 7d: Higher / Lower (CamPom Edition)
Dead-simple, addictive streak game: "Who had the higher CamPom — Player A or Player B?" One round = two random eligible players; correct guess advances, wrong guess ends the run; track best streak in `localStorage`.

- [ ] **Round generation** — sample two players from the eligible pool (reuse the 7a scope configs: P5-only / starters / all D-I / season). Read `cam_gbpm_v3` (+ percentile) straight from the players surface.
- [ ] **Reveal + streak** — flip to show both CamPom values, animate the winner, increment/reset streak; personal-best persisted client-side.
- [ ] **Variants (optional)** — swap the compared metric (PPG, on/off net, RAPM, usage) for replay variety; each is a column we already serve.
- [ ] Client-only over the existing players data; no new endpoint for v1.

### 7e: Build-a-Roster / Pack Opening
Give the user a CamPom budget and legal archetype-slot constraints, let them draft real players into a 5-man (or full rotation), then score the roster by feeding its aggregate through the `roster_impact_model` to output a projected AdjEM and a "your team would rank #X." This is the `/projected` machinery turned into a game. The "pack opening" framing is the fun onboarding: reveal players as randomized packs (rarity by CamPom percentile — Common → Mythic), then build from what you pull.

- [ ] **Pack opening** — randomized reveal of N players with rarity tiers keyed to CamPom percentile (reuse the tier-chip color scale: Elite/Strong/… → Mythic/Rare/…). Purely cosmetic randomness (vary by session, not a shared daily seed).
- [ ] **Roster builder** — draft into archetype slots with a CamPom budget cap; legal-lineup validation (must field a valid 5; optional bench). Reuse archetype chips + player search.
- [ ] **Scoring** — aggregate the drafted roster into the roster-impact feature vector and run it through `roster_impact_model` (the preseason projection calibrator) for a projected AdjEM + implied national rank. **Dependency**: the model expects a specific roster-aggregate feature shape — either reuse the projection feature-builder path or expose a small "score this hypothetical roster" endpoint (`POST /api/roster/score` with a list of `player_id`s). Note this is the one Phase-7 item that likely needs a new endpoint; everything else is client-only over existing routes.
- [ ] **Shareable result** — final team card (starting 5 by archetype color + projected AdjEM + rank), OG-card friendly.
- [ ] **Trading-card tie-in (stretch)** — the per-player "pull" reuses/becomes the shareable player trading card (archetype-bordered, CamPom headline, rarity tier), which is also a standalone share surface on player pages.

### 7f: Easter Eggs (low effort, high delight)
- [ ] **Konami code → "chaos mode"** — re-skin AdjEM/CamPom tier labels with D&D flavor ("Legendary" … "Commoner") and/or swap archetype badges for dice icons. Purely cosmetic, dismissible.
- [ ] **"Party of the day"** — a small homepage card presenting a random real 5-man lineup from `lineup_aggregates` as a D&D adventuring party ("1 Wizard, 2 Fighters, a Cleric, a Barbarian — Adjusted Net +18.2"), deep-linking to `/lineups`.
- [ ] **The record books (`/legends`)** — surface the extremes already in the DB: highest single-season CamPom, biggest on/off swing, most lopsided game the model got wrong, the "purest" archetype (highest `primary_score`). All reads.
- [ ] **D&D loading flavor text** — rotate themed quips during React Query fetches ("Consulting the ancient scrolls of possession data…").
